 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * worker-approval-port.ts — a RESTRICTED view of the authoritative approval
     3	 * ledger for isolated coordination worker processes.
     4	 *
     5	 * A subagent worker runs as its own OS process, so it cannot receive the
     6	 * parent's in-memory `ApprovalStore`. It needs the durable ledger to request
     7	 * and observe tool-level approvals — but handing a child a full `ApprovalStore`
     8	 * would let it call `resolve(...)` and APPROVE ITS OWN REQUEST, which is
     9	 * authority escalation (a compromised worker could grant itself any
    10	 * capability).
    11	 *
    12	 * This port exposes exactly the operations a worker legitimately needs and
    13	 * nothing else:
    14	 *
    15	 *   allowed   read a record by id / binding key / pending capability
    16	 *             create-or-reuse a pending request bound to this worker
    17	 *             atomically consume an APPROVED record it is authorized to use
    18	 *             report its own execution outcome
    19	 *
    20	 *   forbidden approve / deny / revoke / invalidate / group resolution /
    21	 *             mutating another worker's record
    22	 *
    23	 * Consumption is NOT reimplemented here: `consumeApproved` runs inside the
    24	 * store's cross-process lock and re-validates status, expiry, binding key and
    25	 * invocation under that lock, so two processes cannot both consume one
    26	 * single-use approval. The port is a narrowing layer, never a second authority:
    27	 * it always delegates to a store instance opened on the SAME workspace.
    28	 *
    29	 * Decisions stay operator-controlled: `resolve`, `resolveWithEvidence`,
    30	 * `resolveGroup`, `revoke` and `invalidate` are not reachable, so no method on
    31	 * this port can move a request to `approved`. A worker CAN report the outcome of
    32	 * an execution it performed, but only for a record it owns — see
    33	 * `recordExecutionOutcome` below.
    34	 */
    35	
    36	import type { ApprovalStore } from "../../governance/approvals/approval-store.js";
    37	import type { ApprovalRecord } from "../../governance/approvals/approval-types.js";
    38	/** The operations a worker process may perform against the ledger. */
    39	export interface WorkerApprovalPort {
    40	  /** Current durable record, or undefined when it does not exist. */
    41	  get(id: string): ApprovalRecord | undefined;
    42	  /** Authoritative re-read from the ledger (cross-process visibility). */
    43	  loadFresh(id: string): Promise<ApprovalRecord | undefined>;
    44	  /** Look up by the exact binding key an approval was created with. */
    45	  findExact(bindingKey: string): ApprovalRecord | undefined;
    46	  /** A pending approval for this binding key, if one exists. */
    47	  findPendingByBindingKey(bindingKey: string): ApprovalRecord | undefined;
    48	  /** A pending approval for a capability (legacy, non-coordination path). */
    49	  findPending(query: { capability: string }): ApprovalRecord | undefined;
    50	  /**
    51	   * Most recent NON-pending record for a graph/node/capability. Read-only.
    52	   * Needed by the auto-mode branch, which reuses a prior resolution.
    53	   */
    54	  findResolved(opts: { graphId?: string; nodeId?: string; capability?: string }): ApprovalRecord | undefined;
    55	  /**
    56	   * Most recent record for a stable request id (typically the toolCallId).
    57	   *
    58	   * Required by `PolicyGate`'s legacy de-dup branch: after the operator approves,
    59	   * the agent loop re-evaluates the SAME tool call, and without this lookup it
    60	   * mints a second approval instead of honouring the first — the
    61	   * "many pending approvals pile up" failure. Optional chaining at the call site
    62	   * means omitting it here fails SILENTLY, so it must be exposed.
    63	   */
    64	  findByRequestId(requestId: string): ApprovalRecord | undefined;
    65	  /**
    66	   * Create a fresh PENDING approval (legacy capability path).
    67	   *
    68	   * Safe for a worker: it can only ever mint `pending`. Reconciliation of that
    69	   * request requires an operator decision through a path this port does not
    70	   * expose.
    71	   */
    72	  request(opts: Parameters<ApprovalStore["request"]>[0]): Promise<ApprovalRecord>;
    73	  /** Create-or-reuse the pending request for an exact binding. */
    74	  requestOrReusePending(input: Parameters<ApprovalStore["requestOrReusePending"]>[0]): Promise<ApprovalRecord>;
    75	  /** Atomically claim an approved record. Single-use across processes. */
    76	  consumeApproved: ApprovalStore["consumeApproved"];
    77	  /**
    78	   * Report the outcome of an execution THIS worker performed.
    79	   *
    80	   * Scope-limited by the `workerId` bound at construction: the record must
    81	   * belong to this worker, or carry no worker binding at all (legacy
    82	   * non-coordination records). Without this, any worker holding a consumed id
    83	   * could falsify another worker's execution receipt — the store itself
    84	   * validates only status/outcome transition, not reporter identity.
    85	   *
    86	   * Returns the store's own boolean: `true` only when the transition landed.
    87	   */
    88	  recordExecutionOutcome(
    89	    id: string,
    90	    outcome: "succeeded" | "failed" | "cancelled",
    91	  ): Promise<boolean>;
    92	  /** Re-read the ledger so cross-process decisions become visible. */
    93	  load(): Promise<void>;
    94	  /**
    95	   * Classify a still-`pending` consumed claim as `unverified` (terminal,
    96	   * fail-closed). Exposed so `PolicyGate`'s consumed-replay branch can report an
    97	   * exact reason instead of silently degrading to "already consumed".
    98	   */
    99	  markExecutionUnverified?(id: string): Promise<"unverified" | "pending" | "terminal" | "missing">;
   100	}
   101	
   102	/**
   103	 * Open the restricted worker view for an isolated process.
   104	 *
   105	 * This is the ONE sanctioned ApprovalStore construction seam for worker
   106	 * processes (pinned by the R1 boundary freeze): a child cannot receive the
   107	 * parent's in-memory store, so it opens its own instance on the SAME
   108	 * authoritative workspace. Callers pass the workspace they were spawned in —
   109	 * never a config-derived or guessed path — so a worker can never accidentally
   110	 * read a different `.alix` directory.
   111	 *
   112	 * Throws when the ledger cannot be opened or loaded; callers must treat that
   113	 * as "no approval authority" and keep gated operations denied.
   114	 */
   115	export async function openWorkerApprovalPort(
   116	  workspace: string,
   117	  options: { eventLog?: unknown; workerId?: string } = {},
   118	): Promise<WorkerApprovalPort> {
   119	  const { ApprovalStore } = await import("../../governance/approvals/approval-store.js");
   120	  const store = new ApprovalStore(
   121	    workspace,
   122	    options.eventLog ? { eventLog: options.eventLog as never } : {},
   123	  );
   124	  await store.load();
   125	  return createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { ...(options.workerId ? { workerId: options.workerId } : {}) });
   126	}
   127	
   128	/** Methods that would let a worker authorize its own operations. */
   129	const FORBIDDEN = [
   130	  "resolve",
   131	  "resolveWithEvidence",
   132	  "resolveGroup",
   133	  "revoke",
   134	  "invalidate",
   135	  "append",
   136	  "mutate",
   137	] as const;
   138	
   139	/**
   140	 * Build the restricted worker view over a store instance opened on the worker's
   141	 * own authoritative workspace.
   142	 *
   143	 * The returned object is a plain facade with only the allowed methods, so a
   144	 * worker's tool executor and PolicyGate behave exactly as they do in the parent
   145	 * without ever holding a decision-recording handle.
   146	 */
   147	export function createWorkerApprovalPort(
   148	  store: ApprovalStore,
   149	  options: { workerId?: string } = {},
   150	): WorkerApprovalPort {
   151	  const boundWorkerId = options.workerId;
   152	  const port: WorkerApprovalPort = {
   153	    get: (id) => store.get(id),
   154	    loadFresh: (id) => store.loadFresh(id),
   155	    findExact: (bindingKey) => store.findExact(bindingKey),
   156	    findPendingByBindingKey: (bindingKey) => store.findPendingByBindingKey(bindingKey),
   157	    findPending: (query) => store.findPending(query),
   158	    findResolved: (opts) => store.findResolved(opts),
   159	    findByRequestId: (requestId) => store.findByRequestId(requestId),
   160	    request: (opts) => store.request(opts),
   161	    requestOrReusePending: (input) => store.requestOrReusePending(input),
   162	    consumeApproved: store.consumeApproved.bind(store),
   163	    recordExecutionOutcome: async (id, outcome) => {
   164	      // Ownership check FIRST: a worker may only report on a record it owns, or
   165	      // on a legacy record with no worker binding. A port constructed without a
   166	      // workerId therefore refuses to report on worker-bound records at all
   167	      // (fail closed) while still serving legacy records.
   168	      const record = store.get(id) ?? await store.loadFresh(id);
   169	      if (!record) return false;
   170	      if (record.workerId && record.workerId !== boundWorkerId) return false;
   171	      return store.recordExecutionOutcome(id, outcome);
   172	    },
   173	    load: () => store.load(),
   174	    markExecutionUnverified: (id) => store.markExecutionUnverified(id),
   175	  };
   176	
   177	  // Structural guarantee, not a convention: no forbidden method is reachable
   178	  // from the object a worker receives. A future refactor that added one would
   179	  // fail this check immediately.
   180	  for (const method of FORBIDDEN) {
   181	    if (method in port) {
   182	      throw new Error(`WorkerApprovalPort must not expose "${method}"`);
   183	    }
   184	  }
   185	  return port;
   186	}
     1	import { afterEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
     2	import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
     3	import { join } from 'node:path';
     4	import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
     5	import { ApprovalStore } from '../../src/governance/approvals/approval-store.js';
     6	import { createWorkerApprovalPort, type WorkerApprovalPort } from '../../src/governance/approvals/worker-approval-port.js';
     7	import { closeAllSharedLedgers } from '../../src/runtime-state/storage/runtime-ledger.js';
     8	
     9	const cwds: string[] = [];
    10	afterEach(async () => {
    11	  closeAllSharedLedgers();
    12	  for (const cwd of cwds.splice(0)) await rm(cwd, { recursive: true, force: true });
    13	});
    14	
    15	async function workspace() {
    16	  const cwd = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), '.alix-f8b-'));
    17	  cwds.push(cwd);
    18	  const store = new ApprovalStore(cwd);
    19	  await store.load();
    20	  return { cwd, store };
    21	}
    22	
    23	/** A second store instance on the same workspace = another process. */
    24	async function otherProcess(cwd: string) {
    25	  const store = new ApprovalStore(cwd);
    26	  await store.load();
    27	  return store;
    28	}
    29	
    30	const REQUEST = {
    31	  reason: 'worker wants to run a command',
    32	  capability: 'shell.exec',
    33	  bindingKey: 'binding-abc',
    34	  requestFingerprint: 'shell.exec',
    35	  policyRevision: 'rev-1',
    36	  capabilities: ['shell.exec'],
    37	  coordinationRunId: 'run-1',
    38	  workerId: 'worker-a',
    39	  workerAttempt: 0,
    40	  sessionId: 's1',
    41	};
    42	
    43	/**
    44	 * F8b — durable tool-level approval for an ISOLATED worker process.
    45	 *
    46	 * A subagent worker is its own OS process and cannot receive the parent's
    47	 * in-memory store. It opens its own instance on the SAME authoritative
    48	 * workspace and sees the ledger through a RESTRICTED port, so it can request,
    49	 * observe and consume an approval but can never record a decision (which would
    50	 * let it approve its own request).
    51	 */
    52	describe('worker approval port (F8b)', () => {
    53	  it('exposes every operation a worker needs', async () => {
    54	    const { store } = await workspace();
    55	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
    56	    for (const method of [
    57	      'get', 'loadFresh', 'findExact', 'findPendingByBindingKey', 'findPending',
    58	      'findResolved', 'findByRequestId', 'request', 'requestOrReusePending',
    59	      'consumeApproved', 'recordExecutionOutcome', 'load', 'markExecutionUnverified',
    60	    ]) {
    61	      expect(typeof (port as unknown as Record<string, unknown>)[method]).toBe('function');
    62	    }
    63	  });
    64	
    65	  it('reuses a prior approved resolution for the same request id (legacy de-dup)', async () => {
    66	    // PolicyGate's legacy branch looks up the prior record by requestId after the
    67	    // agent loop re-evaluates the same tool call. With the method omitted, that
    68	    // lookup silently returns undefined and a SECOND approval is minted for the
    69	    // same call — the pending-approval pile-up failure.
    70	    const { store } = await workspace();
    71	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-a' });
    72	
    73	    const created = await port.request({
    74	      reason: 'legacy ask', capability: 'shell.exec',
    75	      sessionId: 's1', agentId: 'worker-a', requestId: 'call-1',
    76	    });
    77	    expect(port.findByRequestId('call-1')?.id).toBe(created.id);
    78	    // A different request id must not collide.
    79	    expect(port.findByRequestId('call-2')).toBeUndefined();
    80	  });
    81	
    82	  it('CANNOT approve or deny: no decision method is reachable', async () => {
    83	    const { store } = await workspace();
    84	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store) as unknown as Record<string, unknown>;
    85	    for (const forbidden of ['resolve', 'resolveWithEvidence', 'resolveGroup', 'revoke', 'invalidate', 'mutate']) {
    86	      expect(port[forbidden]).toBeUndefined();
    87	    }
    88	  });
    89	
    90	  it('a worker cannot self-approve its own request through the port', async () => {
    91	    const { store } = await workspace();
    92	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
    93	
    94	    const created = await port.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
    95	    expect(created.status).toBe('pending');
    96	
    97	    // The only mutation surface is consumption, and it refuses a non-approved
    98	    // record. There is no way to move pending -> approved from here.
    99	    const attempt = await port.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   100	    expect(attempt.consumed).toBe(false);
   101	
   102	    const after = await port.loadFresh(created.id);
   103	    expect(after?.status).toBe('pending');
   104	  });
   105	
   106	  it('observes an operator decision made by a DIFFERENT process', async () => {
   107	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   108	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   109	    const created = await port.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   110	
   111	    // The operator resolves through their own store instance (the TUI).
   112	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   113	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   114	
   115	    const observed = await port.loadFresh(created.id);
   116	    expect(observed?.status).toBe('approved');
   117	    expect(observed?.decidedAt).toBeTruthy();
   118	  });
   119	
   120	  it('consumes exactly once across processes (no double effect)', async () => {
   121	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   122	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   123	    const created = await port.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   124	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   125	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   126	
   127	    const first = await port.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   128	    expect(first.consumed).toBe(true);
   129	
   130	    // A second process holding its OWN store must lose the race.
   131	    const rival = await otherProcess(cwd);
   132	    const second = await rival.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   133	    expect(second.consumed).toBe(false);
   134	  });
   135	
   136	  it('rejects consumption bound to a different binding key', async () => {
   137	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   138	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   139	    const created = await port.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   140	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   141	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   142	
   143	    const wrong = await port.consumeApproved(created.id, 'some-other-binding', {});
   144	    expect(wrong.consumed).toBe(false);
   145	    expect((await port.loadFresh(created.id))?.status).toBe('approved');
   146	  });
   147	
   148	  it('an operator DENIAL is visible to the worker and blocks consumption', async () => {
   149	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   150	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   151	    const created = await port.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   152	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   153	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'denied', 'operator');
   154	
   155	    expect((await port.loadFresh(created.id))?.status).toBe('denied');
   156	    const attempt = await port.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   157	    expect(attempt.consumed).toBe(false);
   158	  });
   159	
   160	  it('does not expose another worker\u2019s records through a different binding', async () => {
   161	    const { store } = await workspace();
   162	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   163	    await port.requestOrReusePending({ ...REQUEST, bindingKey: 'worker-b-binding', workerId: 'worker-b' });
   164	
   165	    expect(port.findExact('worker-b-binding')).toBeDefined();
   166	    expect(port.findExact('binding-abc')).toBeUndefined();
   167	  });
   168	
   169	  it('reports the execution outcome of its OWN consumed approval', async () => {
   170	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   171	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-a' });
   172	    const created = await port.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   173	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   174	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   175	    await port.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   176	
   177	    expect(await port.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'succeeded')).toBe(true);
   178	    const after = await port.loadFresh(created.id);
   179	    expect(after?.executionOutcome).toBe('succeeded');
   180	  });
   181	
   182	  it('REFUSES to report an outcome for another worker\u2019s claim', async () => {
   183	    // The store validates only status/outcome-transition, not reporter identity,
   184	    // so the port must refuse a cross-worker write or one worker could falsify
   185	    // another's execution receipt.
   186	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   187	    const ownerPort = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-a' });
   188	    const otherPort = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-b' });
   189	
   190	    const created = await ownerPort.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   191	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   192	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   193	    await ownerPort.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   194	
   195	    expect(await otherPort.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'succeeded')).toBe(false);
   196	    expect((await otherPort.loadFresh(created.id))?.executionOutcome).toBe('pending');
   197	
   198	    // The rightful owner can still report.
   199	    expect(await ownerPort.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'failed')).toBe(true);
   200	  });
   201	
   202	  it('a port with no bound worker id cannot report on a worker-bound claim', async () => {
   203	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   204	    const unbound = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   205	    const bound = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-a' });
   206	
   207	    const created = await bound.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   208	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   209	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   210	    await bound.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   211	
   212	    expect(await unbound.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'succeeded')).toBe(false);
   213	    expect(await bound.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'succeeded')).toBe(true);
   214	  });
   215	
   216	  it('refuses to construct a port that would expose a decision method', async () => {
   217	    // Structural guarantee: if a future refactor added `resolve` to the facade,
   218	    // createWorkerApprovalPort must throw rather than quietly ship it.
   219	    const { store } = await workspace();
   220	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store) as unknown as Record<string, unknown>;
   221	    expect('resolve' in port).toBe(false);
   222	    expect('revoke' in port).toBe(false);
   223	  });
   224	
   225	  it('PolicyGate reuses an approved request through the port instead of asking again', async () => {
   226	    // End-to-end form of the de-dup regression: the legacy gate branch must see
   227	    // the prior approval via findByRequestId. Without it, the agent loop's
   228	    // re-evaluation mints a second pending approval and the tool never runs.
   229	    const { PolicyGate } = await import('../../src/governance/policy/policy-gate.js');
   230	    const { store } = await workspace();
   231	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-a' });
   232	
   233	    const config = {
   234	      version: 1,
   235	      model: { provider: 't', name: 't' },
   236	      permissions: {
   237	        default: 'ask', tools: { 'shell.run': 'ask' }, protectedPaths: [],
   238	        allowNetworkDomains: [], denyCommands: [], sessionMode: 'ask' as const,
   239	      },
   240	      context: { repoMap: false, repoMapMode: 'lite', maxRepoMapTokens: 0, semanticSearch: false, includeGitStatus: false, pinnedFiles: [] },
   241	      runtime: { provider: 'process', shell: 'bash', commandTimeoutMs: 1000, envAllowlist: [] },
   242	      ui: { enabled: false, host: '127.0.0.1', port: 0, transport: 'sse' },
   243	    } as never;
   244	
   245	    const gate = new PolicyGate(config as never, { approvalStore: port as never });
   246	    const request = {
   247	      requestId: 'call-1', toolName: 'shell.run', capability: 'shell.exec',
   248	      args: { command: 'ls' }, cwd: '/tmp', sessionMode: 'ask' as const,
   249	      sessionId: 's1', agentId: 'worker-a', source: 'tool' as const,
   250	    };
   251	
   252	    const first = await gate.evaluateToolCall(request as never);
   253	    expect(first.decision).toBe('ask');
   254	    expect(first.approvalId).toBeTruthy();
   255	
   256	    await store.resolve(first.approvalId!, 'approved', 'operator');
   257	
   258	    const second = await gate.evaluateToolCall(request as never);
   259	    expect(second.decision).toBe('allow');
   260	    expect(second.approvalId).toBe(first.approvalId);
   261	  });
   262	});
     1	/**
     2	 * gate-e-qualification-record.mjs — validate a Gate E execution/security record.
     3	 *
     4	 * WHY THIS EXISTS
     5	 *
     6	 * Gate E asks for five things — "F8b, authority, crash/recovery, ownership and
     7	 * denied-action negatives qualified independently" — and the acceptance matrix
     8	 * has never had a record to check. The failure mode is not "no record". It is a
     9	 * record that counts a test file as a qualified control, or drops the word
    10	 * "independently" because the tests pass.
    11	 *
    12	 * So this validator is deliberately hostile in three specific ways:
    13	 *   - every clause must be present; four of five is a HOLD, not 80%
    14	 *   - every clause must cite evidence files that EXIST
    15	 *   - every clause must state the NEGATIVE case, because a positive assertion
    16	 *     does not catch the regression the control exists to prevent
    17	 *   - declared gaps are preserved, never counted as covered
    18	 *   - independence is a separate axis. Author-side tests cannot close it, and
    19	 *     the outcome label says so instead of hiding it in a footnote.
    20	 *
    21	 * What this cannot do: create a second party. `QUALIFIED-AUTHOR-ONLY` is the
    22	 * honest ceiling for a record this repository can produce alone.
    23	 *
    24	 * Usage:
    25	 *   node benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs <record.json>
    26	 *
    27	 * Exit codes distinguish "the record is sound" from "the gate is cleared", which
    28	 * are different questions:
    29	 *   0 = qualified INDEPENDENTLY (gate closed)
    30	 *   3 = sound record, author-only coverage (gate NOT closed)
    31	 *   1 = HOLD, 2 = usage error
    32	 *
    33	 * Collapsing 3 into 0 would let a CI step read a passing exit code as a cleared
    34	 * security gate. The distinction is the whole point of the label.
    35	 */
    36	
    37	import { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
    38	import { dirname, resolve } from 'node:path';
    39	import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
    40	
    41	/**
    42	 * The five clauses of Gate E, frozen from PRD §9.
    43	 * An unknown clause is a validation error, not an extra credit.
    44	 */
    45	export const GATE_E_CLAUSES = ['F8b', 'authority', 'crash/recovery', 'ownership', 'denied-action negatives'];
    46	
    47	/**
    48	 * Independence classes. There is no `independent` value in this vocabulary:
    49	 * a record cannot certify its own independence, so a record that claimed it
    50	 * would have to be trusted on the one point it cannot establish.
    51	 */
    52	export const INDEPENDENCE_CLASSES = ['author-only', 'model-independent', 'declared-independent'];
    53	
    54	/** Per-clause observation states. */
    55	export const COMPONENT_STATES = ['qualified', 'partial', 'unqualified'];
    56	
    57	/** Gate E outcomes. Independence upgrades the label; it never upgrades coverage. */
    58	export const GATE_E_OUTCOMES = {
    59	  independent: 'QUALIFIED-INDEPENDENT',
    60	  authorOnly: 'QUALIFIED-AUTHOR-ONLY',
    61	  hold: 'HOLD',
    62	};
    63	
    64	/** Where a record's cited paths resolve from. Explicit, never guessed. */
    65	export const PATH_BASES = ['record', 'repo-root'];
    66	
    67	/**
    68	 * Resolve a cited path.
    69	 *
    70	 * A record must SAY which base it uses (`pathBase`), because a silent guess
    71	 * either rejects valid citations or accepts nonexistent ones. `record` resolves
    72	 * beside the record file; `repo-root` resolves from the process cwd, which is
    73	 * the repository root when the documented command is used.
    74	 */
    75	function resolveCited(cited, baseDir, pathBase) {
    76	  return pathBase === 'repo-root' ? resolve(cited) : resolve(baseDir, cited);
    77	}
    78	
    79	function nonEmpty(value) {
    80	  return typeof value === 'string' && value.trim().length > 0;
    81	}
    82	
    83	/**
    84	 * Validate a Gate E qualification record.
    85	 *
    86	 * `problems` are reasons the record cannot be used at all.
    87	 * `declaredGaps` are accepted limitations and never produce a problem — they are
    88	 * the opposite: hiding one WOULD.
    89	 */
    90	export function validateGateERecord(record, options = {}) {
    91	  const baseDir = options.baseDir ? resolve(options.baseDir) : process.cwd();
    92	  const problems = [];
    93	  const notes = [];
    94	
    95	  if (!record || typeof record !== 'object' || Array.isArray(record)) {
    96	    return {
    97	      ok: false,
    98	      problems: ['record must be an object'],
    99	      notes,
   100	      summary: null,
   101	      outcome: GATE_E_OUTCOMES.hold,
   102	    };
   103	  }
   104	
   105	  // ── Artifact pin ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   106	  const artifact = record.artifact;
   107	  if (!artifact || typeof artifact !== 'object' || Array.isArray(artifact)) {
   108	    problems.push('artifact is required: a qualification must name the commit it qualified');
   109	  } else if (!/^[0-9a-f]{40}$/.test(String(artifact.commit ?? ''))) {
   110	    problems.push('artifact.commit must be a full 40-character commit sha');
   111	  }
   112	  if (!Number.isFinite(Date.parse(record.observedAt))) {
   113	    problems.push('observedAt must be an ISO timestamp: a stale record is not a qualification');
   114	  }
   115	
   116	  // A record must declare how its citations resolve. Guessing is how a path
   117	  // check becomes decorative: it either rejects valid citations or accepts
   118	  // nonexistent ones, and both look like a working check.
   119	  const pathBase = record.pathBase;
   120	  if (!PATH_BASES.includes(pathBase)) {
   121	    problems.push(
   122	      `pathBase must be one of ${PATH_BASES.join('|')} and must be explicit: cited paths resolve either beside the record ("record") or from the repository root ("repo-root")`,
   123	    );
   124	  }
   125	
   126	  // ── Runs ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   127	  // A record with no runs is a description of intent. It must carry what was
   128	  // actually executed, with counts, so a failing suite cannot hide behind prose.
   129	  const runs = Array.isArray(record.runs) ? record.runs : null;
   130	  if (!runs || runs.length === 0) {
   131	    problems.push('runs is required: a qualification must record what was executed, not what exists');
   132	  } else {
   133	    let totalTests = 0;
   134	    let totalPassed = 0;
   135	    for (const [index, run] of runs.entries()) {
   136	      const at = `runs[${index}]`;
   137	      if (!run || typeof run !== 'object') {
   138	        problems.push(`${at} is not an object`);
   139	        continue;
   140	      }
   141	      if (!nonEmpty(run.runner)) problems.push(`${at}.runner is required`);
   142	      if (!Array.isArray(run.files) || run.files.length === 0) {
   143	        problems.push(`${at}.files is required: name the files that ran`);
   144	      } else {
   145	        for (const file of run.files) {
   146	          if (!nonEmpty(file)) {
   147	            problems.push(`${at}.files contains an empty path`);
   148	          } else if (pathBase && !existsSync(resolveCited(file, baseDir, pathBase))) {
   149	            problems.push(`${at}.files cites a path that does not exist: ${file}`);
   150	          }
   151	        }
   152	      }
   153	      if (!Number.isInteger(run.tests) || run.tests <= 0) {
   154	        problems.push(`${at}.tests must be a positive integer`);
   155	      }
   156	      if (!Number.isInteger(run.passed) || run.passed < 0) {
   157	        problems.push(`${at}.passed must be a non-negative integer`);
   158	      }
   159	      if (run.failed !== 0) {
   160	        problems.push(`${at}.failed is ${String(run.failed)}: a qualification cannot be built on a failing suite`);
   161	      }
   162	      if (!Number.isInteger(run.passed) || (Number.isInteger(run.tests) && Number.isInteger(run.passed) && run.passed !== run.tests)) {
   163	        problems.push(`${at}: passed (${String(run.passed)}) does not equal tests (${String(run.tests)})`);
   164	      }
   165	      if (Number.isInteger(run.tests)) totalTests += run.tests;
   166	      if (Number.isInteger(run.passed)) totalPassed += run.passed;
   167	    }
   168	    if (totalTests > 0) notes.push(`observed: ${totalPassed}/${totalTests} tests passed in this run`);
   169	  }
   170	
   171	  // ── Components: all five clauses, each with a negative case and real evidence
   172	  const components = Array.isArray(record.components) ? record.components : null;
   173	  const seen = new Set();
   174	  const counts = { qualified: 0, partial: 0, unqualified: 0 };
   175	  if (!components) {
   176	    problems.push('components must be an array: Gate E is five clauses, not one verdict');
   177	  } else {
   178	    for (const [index, component] of components.entries()) {
   179	      const at = `components[${index}]`;
   180	      if (!component || typeof component !== 'object') {
   181	        problems.push(`${at} is not an object`);
   182	        continue;
   183	      }
   184	      const clause = component.clause;
   185	      if (!GATE_E_CLAUSES.includes(clause)) {
   186	        // A near-miss is the interesting failure: "denied action" vs
   187	        // "denied-action negatives" would silently drop a required clause.
   188	        problems.push(`${at} has an unknown Gate E clause: ${String(clause)}`);
   189	        continue;
   190	      }
   191	      if (seen.has(clause)) problems.push(`clause ${clause} is recorded more than once`);
   192	      seen.add(clause);
   193	
   194	      if (!COMPONENT_STATES.includes(component.observed)) {
   195	        problems.push(`${clause} has an invalid observed state: ${String(component.observed)}`);
   196	      } else {
   197	        counts[component.observed] += 1;
   198	      }
   199	
   200	      if (!nonEmpty(component.question)) {
   201	        problems.push(`${clause} has no question: a control must state what it is being asked to prove`);
   202	      }
   203	
   204	      // The negative case is the whole point of a security control. Without it
   205	      // the record asserts that something works, not that removing it would fail.
   206	      if (!nonEmpty(component.negativeCase)) {
   207	        problems.push(
   208	          `${clause} does not state its negative case: a positive assertion does not catch the regression the control exists to prevent`,
   209	        );
   210	      }
   211	
   212	      if (!Array.isArray(component.evidence) || component.evidence.length === 0) {
   213	        problems.push(`${clause} cites no evidence files`);
   214	      } else {
   215	        for (const file of component.evidence) {
   216	          if (!nonEmpty(file)) {
   217	            problems.push(`${clause} cites an empty evidence path`);
   218	          } else if (pathBase && !existsSync(resolveCited(file, baseDir, pathBase))) {
   219	            problems.push(`${clause} cites a path that does not exist: ${file}`);
   220	          }
   221	        }
   222	      }
   223	    }
   224	    for (const clause of GATE_E_CLAUSES) {
   225	      if (!seen.has(clause)) problems.push(`clause ${clause} has no entry: Gate E requires all five`);
   226	    }
   227	  }
   228	
   229	  // ── Declared gaps ───────────────────────────────────────────────
   230	  // Presence is required. An empty list is legitimate; an ABSENT list usually
   231	  // means gaps were dropped rather than none found.
   232	  if (!Array.isArray(record.declaredGaps)) {
   233	    problems.push(
   234	      'declaredGaps must be present (may be empty): a record that reports no gaps must say so explicitly, not by omission',
   235	    );
   236	  } else {
   237	    for (const [index, gap] of record.declaredGaps.entries()) {
   238	      if (!gap || typeof gap !== 'object') {
   239	        problems.push(`declaredGaps[${index}] is not an object`);
   240	        continue;
   241	      }
   242	      if (!nonEmpty(gap.id)) problems.push(`declaredGaps[${index}] has no id`);
   243	      if (!nonEmpty(gap.gap)) problems.push(`declaredGaps[${index}] has no gap description`);
   244	    }
   245	    if (record.declaredGaps.length > 0) {
   246	      notes.push(
   247	        `gaps preserved, NOT covered: ${record.declaredGaps.map(g => g.id).join(', ')}`,
   248	      );
   249	    }
   250	  }
   251	
   252	  // ── Independence ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   253	  const independence = record.independence;
   254	  let independenceClass = 'unknown';
   255	  if (!independence || typeof independence !== 'object' || Array.isArray(independence)) {
   256	    problems.push('independence is required: Gate E specifies "qualified independently" and the class must be stated');
   257	  } else {
   258	    if (!INDEPENDENCE_CLASSES.includes(independence.class)) {
   259	      problems.push(`independence.class must be one of ${INDEPENDENCE_CLASSES.join('|')}; got ${String(independence.class)}`);
   260	    } else {
   261	      independenceClass = independence.class;
   262	    }
   263	    if (independenceClass !== 'author-only' && !nonEmpty(independence.note)) {
   264	      problems.push('independence.note is required when independence is claimed: say how it was obtained');
   265	    }
   266	  }
   267	
   268	  // ── Outcome ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   269	  // Coverage and independence are separate axes. A perfect author-side record is
   270	  // still author-only, and the label must carry that rather than implying more.
   271	  const allQualified =
   272	    !!components &&
   273	    seen.size === GATE_E_CLAUSES.length &&
   274	    counts.qualified === GATE_E_CLAUSES.length;
   275	
   276	  let outcome = GATE_E_OUTCOMES.hold;
   277	  if (problems.length === 0 && allQualified) {
   278	    outcome = independenceClass === 'author-only'
   279	      ? GATE_E_OUTCOMES.authorOnly
   280	      : GATE_E_OUTCOMES.independent;
   281	  }
   282	
   283	  if (outcome === GATE_E_OUTCOMES.authorOnly) {
   284	    notes.push(
   285	      'author-side qualification only: Gate E requires independent qualification, and passing tests authored with the code are not a second party',
   286	    );
   287	    notes.push('this does not clear Gate E; it records the coverage an independent qualifier would review');
   288	  }
   289	
   290	  return {
   291	    ok: problems.length === 0,
   292	    problems,
   293	    notes,
   294	    outcome,
   295	    summary: {
   296	      clauses: GATE_E_CLAUSES.length,
   297	      recorded: seen.size,
   298	      ...counts,
   299	      declaredGaps: Array.isArray(record.declaredGaps) ? record.declaredGaps.length : null,
   300	      independence: independenceClass,
   301	      outcome,
   302	    },
   303	  };
   304	}
   305	
   306	/** Render a validated record for an operator to read, never to sign blind. */
   307	export function renderGateEReport(result) {
   308	  const lines = [];
   309	  lines.push(`valid: ${result.ok}`);
   310	  lines.push(`gate E: ${result.outcome}`);
   311	  if (result.summary) {
   312	    const s = result.summary;
   313	    lines.push(
   314	      `clauses: ${s.recorded}/${s.clauses} (qualified ${s.qualified}, partial ${s.partial}, unqualified ${s.unqualified})`,
   315	    );
   316	    lines.push(`independence: ${s.independence}`);
   317	    if (s.declaredGaps !== null) lines.push(`declared gaps: ${s.declaredGaps}`);
   318	  }
   319	  for (const note of result.notes) lines.push(`note: ${note}`);
   320	  if (result.problems.length > 0) {
   321	    lines.push('problems:');
   322	    for (const problem of result.problems) lines.push(`  - ${problem}`);
   323	  }
   324	  return lines.join('\n');
   325	}
   326	
   327	if (process.argv[1] && import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(resolve(process.argv[1])).href) {
   328	  const path = process.argv[2];
   329	  if (!path) {
   330	    process.stderr.write('usage: node benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs <record.json>\n');
   331	    process.exit(2);
   332	  }
   333	  let raw;
   334	  try {
   335	    raw = readFileSync(resolve(path), 'utf-8');
   336	  } catch {
   337	    process.stderr.write(`cannot read Gate E record: ${path}\n`);
   338	    process.exit(2);
   339	  }
   340	  let parsed;
   341	  try {
   342	    parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
   343	  } catch (error) {
   344	    process.stderr.write(`Gate E record is not valid JSON: ${String(error)}\n`);
   345	    process.exit(2);
   346	  }
   347	  const result = validateGateERecord(parsed, { baseDir: dirname(resolve(path)) });
   348	  process.stdout.write(renderGateEReport(result) + '\n');
   349	  // A sound author-only record is NOT a cleared gate. It gets its own exit code
   350	  // so a pipeline cannot mistake "the record validates" for "Gate E closed".
   351	  process.exitCode = !result.ok
   352	    ? 1
   353	    : result.outcome === GATE_E_OUTCOMES.authorOnly
   354	      ? 3
   355	      : 0;
   356	}

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs | head -n 135; nl -ba tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts | sed -n '130,320p'; nl -ba docs/audits/2026-10-24-gate-e-qualification.md; cat package.json | head -n 140; rg --files -g '*vitest*config*' -g '*gate-e*'" in /home/babasola/Projects/ALiX
 succeeded in 152ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * gate-e-qualification-record.mjs — validate a Gate E execution/security record.
     3	 *
     4	 * WHY THIS EXISTS
     5	 *
     6	 * Gate E asks for five things — "F8b, authority, crash/recovery, ownership and
     7	 * denied-action negatives qualified independently" — and the acceptance matrix
     8	 * has never had a record to check. The failure mode is not "no record". It is a
     9	 * record that counts a test file as a qualified control, or drops the word
    10	 * "independently" because the tests pass.
    11	 *
    12	 * So this validator is deliberately hostile in three specific ways:
    13	 *   - every clause must be present; four of five is a HOLD, not 80%
    14	 *   - every clause must cite evidence files that EXIST
    15	 *   - every clause must state the NEGATIVE case, because a positive assertion
    16	 *     does not catch the regression the control exists to prevent
    17	 *   - declared gaps are preserved, never counted as covered
    18	 *   - independence is a separate axis. Author-side tests cannot close it, and
    19	 *     the outcome label says so instead of hiding it in a footnote.
    20	 *
    21	 * What this cannot do: create a second party. `QUALIFIED-AUTHOR-ONLY` is the
    22	 * honest ceiling for a record this repository can produce alone.
    23	 *
    24	 * Usage:
    25	 *   node benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs <record.json>
    26	 *
    27	 * Exit codes distinguish "the record is sound" from "the gate is cleared", which
    28	 * are different questions:
    29	 *   0 = qualified INDEPENDENTLY (gate closed)
    30	 *   3 = sound record, author-only coverage (gate NOT closed)
    31	 *   1 = HOLD, 2 = usage error
    32	 *
    33	 * Collapsing 3 into 0 would let a CI step read a passing exit code as a cleared
    34	 * security gate. The distinction is the whole point of the label.
    35	 */
    36	
    37	import { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
    38	import { dirname, resolve } from 'node:path';
    39	import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
    40	
    41	/**
    42	 * The five clauses of Gate E, frozen from PRD §9.
    43	 * An unknown clause is a validation error, not an extra credit.
    44	 */
    45	export const GATE_E_CLAUSES = ['F8b', 'authority', 'crash/recovery', 'ownership', 'denied-action negatives'];
    46	
    47	/**
    48	 * Independence classes. There is no `independent` value in this vocabulary:
    49	 * a record cannot certify its own independence, so a record that claimed it
    50	 * would have to be trusted on the one point it cannot establish.
    51	 */
    52	export const INDEPENDENCE_CLASSES = ['author-only', 'model-independent', 'declared-independent'];
    53	
    54	/** Per-clause observation states. */
    55	export const COMPONENT_STATES = ['qualified', 'partial', 'unqualified'];
    56	
    57	/** Gate E outcomes. Independence upgrades the label; it never upgrades coverage. */
    58	export const GATE_E_OUTCOMES = {
    59	  independent: 'QUALIFIED-INDEPENDENT',
    60	  authorOnly: 'QUALIFIED-AUTHOR-ONLY',
    61	  hold: 'HOLD',
    62	};
    63	
    64	/** Where a record's cited paths resolve from. Explicit, never guessed. */
    65	export const PATH_BASES = ['record', 'repo-root'];
    66	
    67	/**
    68	 * Resolve a cited path.
    69	 *
    70	 * A record must SAY which base it uses (`pathBase`), because a silent guess
    71	 * either rejects valid citations or accepts nonexistent ones. `record` resolves
    72	 * beside the record file; `repo-root` resolves from the process cwd, which is
    73	 * the repository root when the documented command is used.
    74	 */
    75	function resolveCited(cited, baseDir, pathBase) {
    76	  return pathBase === 'repo-root' ? resolve(cited) : resolve(baseDir, cited);
    77	}
    78	
    79	function nonEmpty(value) {
    80	  return typeof value === 'string' && value.trim().length > 0;
    81	}
    82	
    83	/**
    84	 * Validate a Gate E qualification record.
    85	 *
    86	 * `problems` are reasons the record cannot be used at all.
    87	 * `declaredGaps` are accepted limitations and never produce a problem — they are
    88	 * the opposite: hiding one WOULD.
    89	 */
    90	export function validateGateERecord(record, options = {}) {
    91	  const baseDir = options.baseDir ? resolve(options.baseDir) : process.cwd();
    92	  const problems = [];
    93	  const notes = [];
    94	
    95	  if (!record || typeof record !== 'object' || Array.isArray(record)) {
    96	    return {
    97	      ok: false,
    98	      problems: ['record must be an object'],
    99	      notes,
   100	      summary: null,
   101	      outcome: GATE_E_OUTCOMES.hold,
   102	    };
   103	  }
   104	
   105	  // ── Artifact pin ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   106	  const artifact = record.artifact;
   107	  if (!artifact || typeof artifact !== 'object' || Array.isArray(artifact)) {
   108	    problems.push('artifact is required: a qualification must name the commit it qualified');
   109	  } else if (!/^[0-9a-f]{40}$/.test(String(artifact.commit ?? ''))) {
   110	    problems.push('artifact.commit must be a full 40-character commit sha');
   111	  }
   112	  if (!Number.isFinite(Date.parse(record.observedAt))) {
   113	    problems.push('observedAt must be an ISO timestamp: a stale record is not a qualification');
   114	  }
   115	
   116	  // A record must declare how its citations resolve. Guessing is how a path
   117	  // check becomes decorative: it either rejects valid citations or accepts
   118	  // nonexistent ones, and both look like a working check.
   119	  const pathBase = record.pathBase;
   120	  if (!PATH_BASES.includes(pathBase)) {
   121	    problems.push(
   122	      `pathBase must be one of ${PATH_BASES.join('|')} and must be explicit: cited paths resolve either beside the record ("record") or from the repository root ("repo-root")`,
   123	    );
   124	  }
   125	
   126	  // ── Runs ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   127	  // A record with no runs is a description of intent. It must carry what was
   128	  // actually executed, with counts, so a failing suite cannot hide behind prose.
   129	  const runs = Array.isArray(record.runs) ? record.runs : null;
   130	  if (!runs || runs.length === 0) {
   131	    problems.push('runs is required: a qualification must record what was executed, not what exists');
   132	  } else {
   133	    let totalTests = 0;
   134	    let totalPassed = 0;
   135	    for (const [index, run] of runs.entries()) {
   130	    // A second process holding its OWN store must lose the race.
   131	    const rival = await otherProcess(cwd);
   132	    const second = await rival.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   133	    expect(second.consumed).toBe(false);
   134	  });
   135	
   136	  it('rejects consumption bound to a different binding key', async () => {
   137	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   138	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   139	    const created = await port.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   140	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   141	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   142	
   143	    const wrong = await port.consumeApproved(created.id, 'some-other-binding', {});
   144	    expect(wrong.consumed).toBe(false);
   145	    expect((await port.loadFresh(created.id))?.status).toBe('approved');
   146	  });
   147	
   148	  it('an operator DENIAL is visible to the worker and blocks consumption', async () => {
   149	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   150	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   151	    const created = await port.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   152	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   153	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'denied', 'operator');
   154	
   155	    expect((await port.loadFresh(created.id))?.status).toBe('denied');
   156	    const attempt = await port.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   157	    expect(attempt.consumed).toBe(false);
   158	  });
   159	
   160	  it('does not expose another worker\u2019s records through a different binding', async () => {
   161	    const { store } = await workspace();
   162	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   163	    await port.requestOrReusePending({ ...REQUEST, bindingKey: 'worker-b-binding', workerId: 'worker-b' });
   164	
   165	    expect(port.findExact('worker-b-binding')).toBeDefined();
   166	    expect(port.findExact('binding-abc')).toBeUndefined();
   167	  });
   168	
   169	  it('reports the execution outcome of its OWN consumed approval', async () => {
   170	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   171	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-a' });
   172	    const created = await port.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   173	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   174	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   175	    await port.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   176	
   177	    expect(await port.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'succeeded')).toBe(true);
   178	    const after = await port.loadFresh(created.id);
   179	    expect(after?.executionOutcome).toBe('succeeded');
   180	  });
   181	
   182	  it('REFUSES to report an outcome for another worker\u2019s claim', async () => {
   183	    // The store validates only status/outcome-transition, not reporter identity,
   184	    // so the port must refuse a cross-worker write or one worker could falsify
   185	    // another's execution receipt.
   186	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   187	    const ownerPort = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-a' });
   188	    const otherPort = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-b' });
   189	
   190	    const created = await ownerPort.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   191	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   192	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   193	    await ownerPort.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   194	
   195	    expect(await otherPort.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'succeeded')).toBe(false);
   196	    expect((await otherPort.loadFresh(created.id))?.executionOutcome).toBe('pending');
   197	
   198	    // The rightful owner can still report.
   199	    expect(await ownerPort.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'failed')).toBe(true);
   200	  });
   201	
   202	  it('a port with no bound worker id cannot report on a worker-bound claim', async () => {
   203	    const { cwd, store } = await workspace();
   204	    const unbound = createWorkerApprovalPort(store);
   205	    const bound = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-a' });
   206	
   207	    const created = await bound.requestOrReusePending(REQUEST);
   208	    const operator = await otherProcess(cwd);
   209	    await operator.resolve(created.id, 'approved', 'operator');
   210	    await bound.consumeApproved(created.id, REQUEST.bindingKey, {});
   211	
   212	    expect(await unbound.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'succeeded')).toBe(false);
   213	    expect(await bound.recordExecutionOutcome(created.id, 'succeeded')).toBe(true);
   214	  });
   215	
   216	  it('refuses to construct a port that would expose a decision method', async () => {
   217	    // Structural guarantee: if a future refactor added `resolve` to the facade,
   218	    // createWorkerApprovalPort must throw rather than quietly ship it.
   219	    const { store } = await workspace();
   220	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store) as unknown as Record<string, unknown>;
   221	    expect('resolve' in port).toBe(false);
   222	    expect('revoke' in port).toBe(false);
   223	  });
   224	
   225	  it('PolicyGate reuses an approved request through the port instead of asking again', async () => {
   226	    // End-to-end form of the de-dup regression: the legacy gate branch must see
   227	    // the prior approval via findByRequestId. Without it, the agent loop's
   228	    // re-evaluation mints a second pending approval and the tool never runs.
   229	    const { PolicyGate } = await import('../../src/governance/policy/policy-gate.js');
   230	    const { store } = await workspace();
   231	    const port = createWorkerApprovalPort(store, { workerId: 'worker-a' });
   232	
   233	    const config = {
   234	      version: 1,
   235	      model: { provider: 't', name: 't' },
   236	      permissions: {
   237	        default: 'ask', tools: { 'shell.run': 'ask' }, protectedPaths: [],
   238	        allowNetworkDomains: [], denyCommands: [], sessionMode: 'ask' as const,
   239	      },
   240	      context: { repoMap: false, repoMapMode: 'lite', maxRepoMapTokens: 0, semanticSearch: false, includeGitStatus: false, pinnedFiles: [] },
   241	      runtime: { provider: 'process', shell: 'bash', commandTimeoutMs: 1000, envAllowlist: [] },
   242	      ui: { enabled: false, host: '127.0.0.1', port: 0, transport: 'sse' },
   243	    } as never;
   244	
   245	    const gate = new PolicyGate(config as never, { approvalStore: port as never });
   246	    const request = {
   247	      requestId: 'call-1', toolName: 'shell.run', capability: 'shell.exec',
   248	      args: { command: 'ls' }, cwd: '/tmp', sessionMode: 'ask' as const,
   249	      sessionId: 's1', agentId: 'worker-a', source: 'tool' as const,
   250	    };
   251	
   252	    const first = await gate.evaluateToolCall(request as never);
   253	    expect(first.decision).toBe('ask');
   254	    expect(first.approvalId).toBeTruthy();
   255	
   256	    await store.resolve(first.approvalId!, 'approved', 'operator');
   257	
   258	    const second = await gate.evaluateToolCall(request as never);
   259	    expect(second.decision).toBe('allow');
   260	    expect(second.approvalId).toBe(first.approvalId);
   261	  });
   262	});
     1	# Gate E — execution/security qualification, author-side
     2	
     3	**Date:** 2026-10-24
     4	**Artifact:** `ea863a25b5dd0e014d275bc1c4b979b7b7d5afee`
     5	**Record:** [gate-e-qualification-record.json](2026-10-24-gate-e-qualification-record.json)
     6	**Validator:** [`benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs`](../../benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs)
     7	**Outcome:** `QUALIFIED-AUTHOR-ONLY` — **the gate is not cleared.**
     8	
     9	## 1. What the gate asks, and what this does
    10	
    11	> "Gate E — Execution/security: F8b, authority, crash/recovery, ownership and
    12	> denied-action negatives qualified independently." — PRD §9
    13	
    14	Five clauses. Before this record, Gate E had never been *run* — the acceptance
    15	matrix said "authorized; independence still absent", and the available evidence was
    16	scattered across two test directories, an invariant register and a historical F8b
    17	report. Nothing assembled it, so nothing could contradict a claim either way.
    18	
    19	This executes the five clauses and records what each one proves, with the negative
    20	case named. It does **not** close Gate E, because one word in the requirement —
    21	*independently* — is not something an author can supply.
    22	
    23	## 2. Clauses, evidence, and what each one actually proves
    24	
    25	| # | Clause | Negative case that would catch a regression | Evidence | State |
    26	|---|---|---|---|---|
    27	| GE-01 | **F8b** — child-process approval surface | `WorkerApprovalPort` throws at construction if `resolve`/`revoke`/`invalidate`/`mutate` appear; `recordExecutionOutcome` scoped to the bound `workerId` | `worker-approval-port.vitest.ts` (14 tests, red-checked both ways) | qualified |
    28	| GE-02 | **Authority** — policy deny survives every mode | Deny stays deny across `ask`/`auto`/`bypass`; no allow is invented; deny-wins when both match; a consumed approval cannot be re-consumed | `pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts`, `policy-gate.test.ts`, `rule-evaluator.test.ts` | qualified |
    29	| GE-03 | **Crash/recovery** — claimed-then-crashed approval stays consumed | A pending claim observed on replay is `unverified` and denied without reissuing authority; a legacy approval with no fingerprint fails closed; silence is never success | `approval-crash-recovery.test.ts`, `legacy-approval-single-use.test.ts`, `approval-consumed-propagation.test.ts` | qualified |
    30	| GE-04 | **Ownership** — no write outside declared scope | The owned-path rule decides every declared-scope write; a broader capability permission never widens the grant; `file.create` backstops new files | `path-scope.test.ts` parity table, `ownership-gate.test.ts`, `ownership-registry.test.ts`, `policy-protected-path-parity.vitest.ts` | qualified |
    31	| GE-05 | **Denied-action negatives** | Protected paths are not written; a denied command never reaches dispatch; denial produces a deny decision, not an execution | `pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts`, `command-classifier.test.ts`, `policy-protected-path-parity.vitest.ts`, `ownership-gate.test.ts` | qualified |
    32	
    33	**Executed:** 282/282 tests passed — 214 via `node --test`, 68 via `vitest run`.
    34	Counts are in the record and checked by the validator: a failing suite, or counts
    35	that do not add up, cannot produce a qualification.
    36	
    37	## 3. Declared gaps — preserved, never counted as covered
    38	
    39	Five named limitations travel with the record. None is new; each was already in the
    40	invariant register or its acceptance row. The validator requires the list to be
    41	*present* (an absent list usually means gaps were dropped rather than none found),
    42	and reports them as `gaps preserved, NOT covered`.
    43	
    44	| ID | Gap | Why it stays open |
    45	|---|---|---|
    46	| F-02 | No explicit wrong-workspace test for the worker port | The failure mode cannot be constructed through the port (no user-supplied workspace path), but the test does not exist |
    47	| F-03 | Tool-level operator denial not exercised through the full TUI scenario | Denial proven at port and dispatch level only |
    48	| F-04 | Ownership **hard-conflict** rejection not demonstrated | Prior evidence shows dependency *serialization*, not rejection of an unsynchronized conflict |
    49	| F-07 | Transient clamp-reload observation unresolved | Possible timing fragility; one unreproduced observation |
    50	| F-11 | D4-04 delegate transport flake bounded, not fixed | 42 consecutive passes, zero reproduction; standing hypothesis unproven |
    51	
    52	GE-05 is therefore "qualified" for the paths it does test and **F-03 is the reason
    53	that is not the same as "all denied actions"**. The record says both.
    54	
    55	## 4. Why the label is `QUALIFIED-AUTHOR-ONLY`
    56	
    57	Gate E's word is *independently*. The tests were authored alongside the code, so
    58	they encode the author's model of the invariant — including the author's blind
    59	spots. This is not a measurement defect to be repaired by more tests; it is the
    60	thing independence names.
    61	
    62	The honest dispositions:
    63	
    64	1. **Independent qualification (best).** A second party runs the five clauses and
    65	   arrives at its own verdict. Converts the label to `QUALIFIED-INDEPENDENT`.
    66	2. **Model-independent review (weaker, labelled).** A different model family, own
    67	   verdict, pinned commit — the same class already labelled throughout Gate B. It
    68	   is not a person, and the record has a distinct value for it rather than
    69	   overloading "independent".
    70	3. **Author-only (this record).** Coverage is real and executable; independence is
    71	   absent.
    72	
    73	An operator waiver records acceptance of author-only testing. It does not create a
    74	second party, and this record claims none.
    75	
    76	## 5. Two validator decisions worth stating
    77	
    78	**Exit `3`, not `0`.** A sound author-only record exits `3`. If it exited `0`, a CI
    79	step would read "the record validates" as "Gate E closed" — the two questions are
    80	different, and the exit code now says so.
    81	
    82	**`pathBase` is required.** The record declares whether its citations resolve beside
    83	the record file or from the repository root. Both are legitimate; guessing is not.
    84	A silent default either rejects valid citations or accepts nonexistent ones, and
    85	either way the path check becomes decorative. (Both Gate B's and Gate F's validators
    86	learned this the same way — by failing on a real record.)
    87	
    88	## 6. Computed state
    89	
    90	```
    91	valid: true
    92	gate E: QUALIFIED-AUTHOR-ONLY
    93	clauses: 5/5 (qualified 5, partial 0, unqualified 0)
    94	independence: author-only
    95	declared gaps: 5
    96	note: observed: 282/282 tests passed in this run
    97	note: gaps preserved, NOT covered: F-02, F-03, F-04, F-07, F-11
    98	note: author-side qualification only: Gate E requires independent qualification
    99	note: this does not clear Gate E; it records the coverage an independent qualifier would review
   100	```
   101	
   102	## 6.1 Suite repair found while verifying this record
   103	
   104	Running the full suite to confirm the record's numbers surfaced 20 failures that
   105	had nothing to do with Gate E, and they were **pre-existing**: both suites fail
   106	identically at parent commit `ea863a25` in a clean worktree.
   107	
   108	The cause was a fixture defect, not a product defect. Two fixtures configured model
   109	tiers under `subagents.<tier>`, but `createModelResolver` reads `config.models[tier]`.
   110	So `getRoleModel` threw "Subagent tier is unconfigured" and every assertion
   111	downstream of `spawn()` observed that error instead of the behaviour it was written
   112	to check.
   113	
   114	Type-checking could not catch it: §2.8.1/§2.8.4 made `subagents.<tier>` a
   115	loader-derived compatibility projection — genuinely part of the type, never
   116	persisted, never the source. The fixtures kept the projection and lost the source.
   117	
   118	| Suite | Before | After |
   119	|---|---|---|
   120	| `subagent-manager.test.ts` | 3/19 | **19/19** |
   121	| `subagent-worker-executor.test.ts` | 9/13 | **13/13** |
   122	
   123	Node lane after repair: **8915 pass, 0 fail**. Both are recorded in the Gate E
   124	record under `suiteRepair`. They are **not** Gate E evidence — the five clauses cite
   125	different files — but a suite reporting 20 failures cannot be read as green, so the
   126	repair is written down rather than left implicit.
   127	
   128	Two tests were *cancelled*, not failed, by resource contention in the parallel run:
   129	`cli-discover.test.js` spent 48 minutes under contention and passes in 11s
   130	standalone, and `ContextCompiler.warm()` timed out at 120s but passes in 2s
   131	standalone. Cancellation is recorded as cancellation.
   132	
   133	## 7. Reproduce
   134	
   135	```sh
   136	node --test dist/tests/security/legacy-approval-single-use.test.js \
   137	  dist/tests/security/approval-crash-recovery.test.js \
   138	  dist/tests/security/approval-consumed-propagation.test.js \
   139	  dist/tests/ownership/path-scope.test.js \
   140	  dist/tests/ownership/ownership-gate.test.js \
   141	  dist/tests/ownership/ownership-registry.test.js \
   142	  dist/tests/policy/policy-gate.test.js \
   143	  dist/tests/policy/rule-evaluator.test.js \
   144	  dist/tests/policy/command-classifier.test.js
   145	npx vitest run --config vitest.config.mts tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts \
   146	  tests/security/policy-protected-path-parity.vitest.ts \
   147	  tests/security/pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts \
   148	  tests/security/pre-r024-isolation-qualification.vitest.ts \
   149	  tests/security/approval-event-dedup.vitest.ts
   150	node benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs docs/audits/2026-10-24-gate-e-qualification-record.json
   151	node --test benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.test.mjs
   152	```
   153	
   154	The record command exits `3` today. That is the correct result and it will keep
   155	being correct until a second party qualifies the five clauses.
   156	
   157	## 8. What this does not claim
   158	
   159	- Not independent qualification. Author-side only, labelled everywhere.
   160	- Not a Gate E clearance. The gate stays open on the word it names.
   161	- Not "all denied actions are caught" — F-03 and F-04 bound that claim.
   162	- Not F8b release clearance. P0-G remains a separate security track (PRD §5, P0-G).
   163	- Not Gate F clearance. Independent clearance and the maintainer decision remain
   164	  absent, and [`release-clearance-record.mjs`](../../benchmark/evidence/release-clearance-record.mjs)
   165	  computes `HOLD`.

src/agents/subagent-cli.ts:973:          // worker's sole owned path so owned-path auto-approval and the patch
     1	/**
     2	 * gate-e-qualification-record.test.mjs — pins the Gate E qualification contract.
     3	 *
     4	 * Gate E has never had a record to check, so the failure modes worth pinning are
     5	 * the ones that would let a record *look* complete:
     6	 *   - four of five clauses recorded, read as "nearly done"
     7	 *   - a test file counted as a qualified control
     8	 *   - a clause with no negative case, i.e. a positive assertion standing in for
     9	 *     the regression the control exists to prevent
    10	 *   - declared gaps quietly omitted rather than declared
    11	 *   - author-only coverage labelled as qualification, dropping "independently"
    12	 *
    13	 * The last one is the whole gate. `QUALIFIED-AUTHOR-ONLY` exists so the honest
    14	 * ceiling has a name and cannot be rounded up.
    15	 */
    16	
    17	import { test } from 'node:test';
    18	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
    19	import { mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
    20	import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
    21	import { join } from 'node:path';
    22	import {
    23	  COMPONENT_STATES,
    24	  GATE_E_CLAUSES,
    25	  GATE_E_OUTCOMES,
    26	  INDEPENDENCE_CLASSES,
    27	  PATH_BASES,
    28	  renderGateEReport,
    29	  validateGateERecord,
    30	} from './gate-e-qualification-record.mjs';
    31	
    32	const COMMIT = 'a'.repeat(40);
    33	
    34	/** Every clause, qualified, with a negative case and one real evidence file. */
    35	function components() {
    36	  return GATE_E_CLAUSES.map(clause => ({
    37	    clause,
    38	    question: `Does ${clause} hold?`,
    39	    observed: 'qualified',
    40	    negativeCase: `removing ${clause} fails the test`,
    41	    evidence: ['evidence.md'],
    42	  }));
    43	}
    44	
    45	function baseRecord(overrides = {}) {
    46	  return {
    47	    artifact: { commit: COMMIT, branch: 'main' },
    48	    observedAt: '2026-10-24T00:00:00.000Z',
    49	    pathBase: 'record',
    50	    runs: [{ runner: 'node --test', files: ['evidence.md'], tests: 10, passed: 10, failed: 0 }],
    51	    components: components(),
    52	    declaredGaps: [],
    53	    independence: { class: 'author-only', note: 'tests authored with the code' },
    54	    ...overrides,
    55	  };
    56	}
    57	
    58	/** Create the temp dir holding every path the record cites. */
    59	function withFixtures(fn) {
    60	  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'gate-e-'));
    61	  try {
    62	    writeFileSync(join(dir, 'evidence.md'), '# evidence\n');
    63	    return fn(dir);
    64	  } finally {
    65	    rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
    66	  }
    67	}
    68	
    69	test('a complete author-side record qualifies at the author-only label', () => {
    70	  withFixtures(dir => {
    71	    const result = validateGateERecord(baseRecord(), { baseDir: dir });
    72	    assert.deepEqual(result.problems, []);
    73	    assert.equal(result.ok, true);
    74	    assert.equal(result.outcome, GATE_E_OUTCOMES.authorOnly);
    75	    assert.equal(result.summary.qualified, GATE_E_CLAUSES.length);
    76	    assert.equal(result.summary.independence, 'author-only');
    77	    const text = renderGateEReport(result);
    78	    assert.ok(/author-side qualification only/.test(text));
    79	    assert.ok(/does not clear Gate E/.test(text));
    80	  });
    81	});
    82	
    83	test('four of five clauses is a HOLD, not 80 percent', () => {
    84	  withFixtures(dir => {
    85	    const result = validateGateERecord(baseRecord({ components: components().slice(0, 4) }), { baseDir: dir });
    86	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
    87	    assert.equal(result.outcome, GATE_E_OUTCOMES.hold);
    88	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /denied-action negatives has no entry/.test(p)));
    89	  });
    90	});
    91	
    92	test('a near-miss clause name is rejected rather than silently accepted', () => {
    93	  withFixtures(dir => {
    94	    const comps = components();
    95	    comps[4] = { ...comps[4], clause: 'denied action' };
    96	    const result = validateGateERecord(baseRecord({ components: comps }), { baseDir: dir });
    97	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
    98	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /unknown Gate E clause/.test(p)));
    99	  });
   100	});
   101	
   102	test('a clause without a negative case is rejected', () => {
   103	  withFixtures(dir => {
   104	    const comps = components();
   105	    delete comps[0].negativeCase;
   106	    const result = validateGateERecord(baseRecord({ components: comps }), { baseDir: dir });
   107	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
   108	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /does not state its negative case/.test(p)));
   109	    assert.equal(result.outcome, GATE_E_OUTCOMES.hold);
   110	  });
   111	});
   112	
   113	test('a partial clause blocks qualification even with the other four qualified', () => {
   114	  withFixtures(dir => {
   115	    const comps = components();
   116	    comps[2].observed = 'partial';
   117	    const result = validateGateERecord(baseRecord({ components: comps }), { baseDir: dir });
   118	    assert.equal(result.ok, true);
   119	    assert.equal(result.summary.partial, 1);
   120	    assert.equal(result.outcome, GATE_E_OUTCOMES.hold);
   121	  });
   122	});
   123	
   124	test('a failing suite cannot be qualified', () => {
   125	  withFixtures(dir => {
   126	    const result = validateGateERecord(
   127	      baseRecord({ runs: [{ runner: 'node --test', files: ['evidence.md'], tests: 10, passed: 9, failed: 1 }] }),
   128	      { baseDir: dir },
   129	    );
   130	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
   131	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /cannot be built on a failing suite/.test(p)));
   132	  });
   133	});
   134	
   135	test('a run whose counts do not add up is rejected', () => {
   136	  withFixtures(dir => {
   137	    const result = validateGateERecord(

   139	      { baseDir: dir },
   140	    );
   141	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
   142	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /does not equal tests/.test(p)));
   143	  });
   144	});
   145	
   146	test('a record with no runs describes intent, not a qualification', () => {
   147	  withFixtures(dir => {
   148	    const result = validateGateERecord(baseRecord({ runs: [] }), { baseDir: dir });
   149	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
   150	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /must record what was executed/.test(p)));
   151	  });
   152	});
   153	
   154	test('cited evidence that does not exist is not evidence', () => {

   162	});
   163	
   164	test('an undeclared path base is rejected rather than guessed', () => {
   165	  withFixtures(dir => {
   166	    const record = baseRecord();

   168	    const result = validateGateERecord(record, { baseDir: dir });
   169	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
   170	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /pathBase must be one of/.test(p)));
   171	  });
   172	});
   173	
   174	test('repo-root citations resolve from the repository root', () => {
   175	  // A real tracked file, so repo-root resolution is exercised for real.
   176	  const result = validateGateERecord(
   177	    baseRecord({
   178	      pathBase: 'repo-root',
   179	      runs: [{ runner: 'node --test', files: ['package.json'], tests: 1, passed: 1, failed: 0 }],
   180	      components: components().map(c => ({ ...c, evidence: ['package.json'] })),
   181	    }),

   183	  );
   184	  assert.deepEqual(result.problems, []);

   186	
   187	test('declaredGaps must be present, though it may be empty', () => {

   194	  });
   195	});
   196	
   197	test('declared gaps are preserved in the report and never counted as covered', () => {
   198	  withFixtures(dir => {
   199	    const result = validateGateERecord(
   200	      baseRecord({ declaredGaps: [{ id: 'F-04', gap: 'hard-conflict rejection not demonstrated' }] }),
   201	      { baseDir: dir },
   202	    );
   203	    assert.equal(result.ok, true);
   204	    assert.equal(result.summary.declaredGaps, 1);
   205	    assert.ok(result.notes.some(n => /gaps preserved, NOT covered: F-04/.test(n)));
   206	    assert.equal(result.summary.qualified, GATE_E_CLAUSES.length);
   207	  });
   208	});
   209	
   210	test('model-independent review is labelled distinctly from independent', () => {
   211	  withFixtures(dir => {
   212	    const result = validateGateERecord(
   213	      baseRecord({ independence: { class: 'model-independent', note: 'reviewed by a different model family' } }),
   214	      { baseDir: dir },
   215	    );
   216	    assert.equal(result.outcome, GATE_E_OUTCOMES.independent);
   217	    // The label is the ceiling, and "independent" is not among the classes a
   218	    // record may declare for itself.
   219	    assert.ok(!INDEPENDENCE_CLASSES.includes('independent'));
   220	  });
   221	});
   222	
   223	test('independence is a required axis, not an optional comment', () => {
   224	  withFixtures(dir => {
   225	    const record = baseRecord();
   226	    delete record.independence;
   227	    const result = validateGateERecord(record, { baseDir: dir });
   228	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
   229	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /independence is required/.test(p)));
   230	    assert.equal(result.outcome, GATE_E_OUTCOMES.hold);
   231	  });
   232	});

   234	test('an unknown independence class is rejected', () => {
   235	  withFixtures(dir => {

   238	      { baseDir: dir },
   239	    );
   240	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
   241	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /independence.class must be one of/.test(p)));
   242	  });
   243	});

   245	test('an abbreviated commit cannot pin a qualification', () => {
   246	  withFixtures(dir => {
   247	    const result = validateGateERecord(baseRecord({ artifact: { commit: COMMIT.slice(0, 8) } }), { baseDir: dir });
   248	    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
   249	    assert.ok(result.problems.some(p => /full 40-character commit sha/.test(p)));
   250	  });
   251	});

   253	test('the vocabularies are frozen and contain no "independent" shortcut', () => {
   254	  assert.deepEqual(GATE_E_CLAUSES, ['F8b', 'authority', 'crash/recovery', 'ownership', 'denied-action negatives']);
   255	  assert.deepEqual(COMPONENT_STATES, ['qualified', 'partial', 'unqualified']);
   256	  assert.deepEqual(PATH_BASES, ['record', 'repo-root']);
   257	  assert.deepEqual(Object.values(GATE_E_OUTCOMES), [
   258	    'QUALIFIED-INDEPENDENT',
   259	    'QUALIFIED-AUTHOR-ONLY',
   260	    'HOLD',
   261	  ]);
   262	  // A record may not declare bare "independent": the class is either author-only
   263	  // or a named weaker form.
   264	  assert.ok(!INDEPENDENCE_CLASSES.includes('independent'));
   265	});
import { defineConfig } from 'vitest/config';

export default defineConfig({
  test: {
    // Run TypeScript vitest files only (not node:test files)
    include: ['tests/**/*.vitest.ts'],
    exclude: ['**/node_modules/**', 'dist/**'],
    coverage: {
      provider: 'v8',
      enabled: false,
    },
    // CI hang mitigation: the linux `unit` job's vitest run was silently
    // hanging 6h on GitHub's ubuntu runner with no output. Root-caused via a
    // cancelled run's log: vitest progressed through test files then stalled
    // at a worker-load boundary (last logged: init-live.vitest.ts, next file
    // reflection.vitest.ts never starts; testTimeout does NOT fire, so it's a
    // fork worker-load deadlock, not a test body). The suite REQUIRES the
    // `forks` pool — threads/vmThreads break 9 fork-isolation-dependent files.
    //
    // Defense in depth:
    // - maxWorkers:2 — caps concurrent fork workers, the vitest-4 Node-24
    //   stabilization (vitest#8968/#8861) without the fileParallelism:false
    //   serial churn that maximized worker spawns.
    // - testTimeout:10000 — a genuinely stalling test now FAILS after 10s with
    //   its name instead of blocking the pool forever.
    // The CI workflow also caps the Vitest step at timeout-minutes:15, so any
    // residual stall fails fast with captured output instead of the 6h silence.
    maxWorkers: 2,
    testTimeout: 10000,
  },
  onConsoleLog: () => {},
