   317	    const toolDecision = this.config.permissions.tools?.[capability];
   318	    if (toolDecision) {
   319	      const effective = applySessionMode(toolDecision, request.sessionMode);
   320	      if (effective === "deny") {
   321	        return { requestId: request.requestId, capability, decision: "deny", reason: `Denied by tool policy (mode: ${request.sessionMode})`, matchedRuleId: `tool-policy-${capability}`, policyRevision };
   322	      }
   323	      // A blanket allow must NOT widen past a declared owned scope: the
   324	      // owned-path rule (5.5) IS the authorization for an owned write, so an
   325	      // allowed capability falls through to it instead of short-circuiting.
   326	      // Returning allow here skipped the rule entirely — the write
   327	      // capability's permission resolves to "allow" under auto/bypass session
   328	      // modes, so a worker with ownedPaths could write anywhere in the
   329	      // workspace (observed on a real gpt-4o coordination run: a worker
   330	      // scoped to one file created two sibling files with no denial).
   331	      if (effective === "allow" && !hasOwnedWriteScope(request)) {
   332	        return { requestId: request.requestId, capability, decision: "allow", reason: `Allowed by tool policy (mode: ${request.sessionMode})`, matchedRuleId: `tool-policy-${capability}`, policyRevision };
   333	      }
   334	    }
   335	
   336	    // 5.5 Owned-path auto-approval (headless write subagents).
   337	    // The subagent's ownedPaths ARE the authorization: a write scoped entirely to
   338	    // owned paths is allowed; a write touching anything outside them is denied.
   339	    if (hasOwnedWriteScope(request)) {
   340	      const targets = mutationTargets(args);
   341	      if (targets.length === 0) {
   342	        // Unscoped (unparseable targets) — fall through to default/ask; headless
   343	        // subagents have no approval store so this fails closed.
   344	      } else {
   345	        const resolvedTargets = targets.map((t) => resolvePolicyPath(request.cwd, t));
   346	        // Protected paths are a hard deny even when owned (patch.apply has no
   347	        // args.path, so step 1 never sees its targets — check them here).
   348	        const protectedTarget = resolvedTargets.find((t) =>
   349	          isProtectedPath(this.config.permissions.protectedPaths, t, workspaceRelative(request.cwd, t)));
   350	        if (protectedTarget) {
   351	          return {
   352	            requestId: request.requestId, capability, decision: "deny",
   353	            reason: `Path protected: ${protectedTarget}`, matchedRuleId: "protected-path-rule", policyRevision,
   354	          };
   355	        }
   356	        if (resolvedTargets.every((t) => isWithinOwnedScope(t, request.ownedPaths!, request.cwd))) {
   357	          return {
   358	            requestId: request.requestId, capability, decision: "allow",
   359	            reason: "Write targets owned path", matchedRuleId: "owned-path-rule", policyRevision,
   360	          };
   361	        }
   362	        const outside = resolvedTargets.filter((t) => !isWithinOwnedScope(t, request.ownedPaths!, request.cwd));
   363	        return {
   364	          requestId: request.requestId, capability, decision: "deny",
   365	          reason: `Write target outside owned paths: ${outside.join(", ")}`, matchedRuleId: "owned-path-rule", policyRevision,
     1	/**
     2	 * policy-loader.ts — Load PolicyRules from disk or fall back to defaults.
     3	 *
     4	 * Reads .alix/policies/*.json and validates each rule.
     5	 * Falls back to built-in defaults when no policy files exist.
     6	 */
     7	
     8	import { readdir, readFile } from "node:fs/promises";
     9	import { join } from "node:path";
    10	import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
    11	import type { PolicyRule } from "./policy-rule.js";
    12	import { validatePolicyRule } from "./policy-rule.js";
    13	import { RuleEvaluator } from "./rule-evaluator.js";
    14	import { defaultPolicyRules } from "./default-policies.js";
    15	
    16	/** Load PolicyRule[] from disk or return defaults. */
    17	export async function loadPolicyRules(cwd: string): Promise<PolicyRule[]> {
    18	  const policiesDir = join(cwd, ".alix", "policies");
    19	  if (!existsSync(policiesDir)) {
    20	    return defaultPolicyRules();
    21	  }
    22	
    23	  const files = await readdir(policiesDir);
    24	  const jsonFiles = files.filter(f => f.endsWith(".json"));
    25	  if (jsonFiles.length === 0) {
    26	    return defaultPolicyRules();
    27	  }
    28	
    29	  const rules: PolicyRule[] = [];
    30	  const seenIds = new Set<string>();
    31	
    32	  for (const f of jsonFiles) {
    33	    try {
    34	      const raw = await readFile(join(policiesDir, f), "utf-8");
    35	      const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
    36	
    37	      // Single rule or array
    38	      const items: unknown[] = Array.isArray(parsed) ? parsed : [parsed];
    39	
    40	      for (const item of items) {
    41	        const rule = item as PolicyRule;
    42	        const validation = validatePolicyRule(rule);
    43	        if (!validation.valid) {
    44	          console.warn(`Skipping invalid policy rule in ${f}: ${validation.errors.join("; ")}`);
    45	          continue;
    46	        }
    47	        if (seenIds.has(rule.id)) {
    48	          console.warn(`Skipping duplicate policy rule ID: ${rule.id} in ${f}`);
    49	          continue;
    50	        }
    51	        seenIds.add(rule.id);
    52	        rules.push(rule);
    53	      }
    54	    } catch (err) {
    55	      console.warn(`Skipping invalid policy file ${f}: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`);
    56	    }
    57	  }
    58	
    59	  return rules.length > 0 ? rules : defaultPolicyRules();
    60	}
    61	
    62	/** Convenience: load rules and wrap in a RuleEvaluator. */
    63	export async function loadRuleEvaluator(cwd: string): Promise<RuleEvaluator> {
    64	  const rules = await loadPolicyRules(cwd);
    65	  return new RuleEvaluator(rules);
    66	}

   195	        // the target between that check and this write.

   217	          // Exclusive creation closes the exists-check/write race: this call
   218	          // can never overwrite a file created by another worker.

   229	                retryable: false,
   230	              };

   779	test("file.create owned-scope parity table (router second net, R1.5)", async () => {
   780	  // The create backstop is the same owned decision as file.delete's and the
   781	  // overwrite net's, so it gets a parity table, not a spot check. A NEW file
   782	  // outside the scope used to be created with no denial at all: the overwrite
   783	  // branch only covered files that already existed.
   784	  const ws = `create-parity-${process.pid}`;
   785	  await mkdir(`/tmp/${ws}/nested`, { recursive: true });
   786	
   787	  const cases: Array<{ owned: string[] | undefined; path: string; allowed: boolean }> = [
   788	    { owned: [`${ws}/**`], path: `${ws}/inside.txt`, allowed: true },
   789	    { owned: [`${ws}/**`], path: `${ws}/nested/deep.txt`, allowed: true },
   790	    { owned: [`${ws}/exact.txt`], path: `${ws}/exact.txt`, allowed: true },
   791	    { owned: [`${ws}/other.txt`], path: `${ws}/exact2.txt`, allowed: false },
   792	    { owned: [`${ws}/**`], path: `${ws}/../outside-${process.pid}.txt`, allowed: false },
   793	    { owned: ["**"], path: `${ws}/inside2.txt`, allowed: true },
   794	    // No declared ownership (operator context) is unaffected.
   795	    { owned: undefined, path: `${ws}/inside3.txt`, allowed: true },
   796	  ];
   797	
   798	  try {
   799	    for (const { owned, path, allowed } of cases) {
   800	      const router = new FileToolRouter("/tmp");
   801	      const result = await router.execute({
   802	        toolCallId: "1",
   803	        name: "file.create",
   804	        args: { path, content: "x" },
   805	        ...(owned ? { ownedPaths: owned } : {}),
   806	      });
   807	      assert.strictEqual(result.kind, allowed ? "success" : "error", `${JSON.stringify(owned)} -> ${path}`);
   808	      const written = await readFile(resolve("/tmp", path), "utf8").then(() => true, () => false);
   809	      assert.strictEqual(written, allowed, `${JSON.stringify(owned)} -> ${path} must ${allowed ? "" : "not "}be written`);
   810	    }
   811	  } finally {
   812	    await rm(`/tmp/${ws}`, { recursive: true, force: true });
   813	    await rm(`/tmp/outside-${process.pid}.txt`, { force: true });
   814	  }
   815	});

     1	/**
     2	 * policy-protected-path-parity.vitest.ts — parity table for the protected-path
     3	 * decision in `PolicyGate`.
     4	 *
     5	 * WHY THIS EXISTS
     6	 *
     7	 * `PolicyGate.isProtectedPath` compared workspace-RELATIVE patterns (`.git/**`,
     8	 * `.env`, `secrets/**` — the shipped defaults) against resolved ABSOLUTE paths.
     9	 * They never matched, so the gate's protected-path rule silently never fired for
    10	 * the default configuration. The file router and patch guard both caught these
    11	 * writes, so nothing escaped in practice — but a policy rule that cannot fire is
    12	 * a policy rule that is not a control.
    13	 *
    14	 * This is an authorization change (it changes what an allow/deny decision
    15	 * authorizes), so per the repo rule it gets an expected-outcome TABLE broader
    16	 * than the implementation, not a spot check of the fixed cases. Every row states
    17	 * its expected verdict so a regression in ANY direction fails, including
    18	 * over-blocking.
    19	 *
    20	 * @module
    21	 */
    22	
    23	import { describe, it, expect } from "vitest";
    24	import { mkdirSync, mkdtempSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
    25	import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
    26	import { join } from "node:path";
    27	import { PolicyGate } from "../../src/governance/policy/policy-gate.js";
    28	import { DEFAULT_CONFIG } from "../../src/operations/config/defaults.js";
    29	
    30	const PATTERNS = DEFAULT_CONFIG.permissions.protectedPaths;
    31	
    32	type Row = {
    33	  /** Path as the model would supply it. */
    34	  target: string;
    35	  expect: "deny" | "allow";
    36	  why: string;
    37	};
    38	
    39	/**
    40	 * The full declared outcome table. Mixed deliberately:
    41	 * - relative + absolute forms of the same protected target must agree
    42	 * - enclosing and sibling names must not be over-blocked
    43	 * - prefix collisions must not match (`src/file.ts` vs `src/file.tsx`)
    44	 */
    45	const ROWS: Row[] = [
    46	  // ── protected targets, relative form ──
    47	  { target: ".git/config", expect: "deny", why: "`.git/**` prefix match" },
    48	  { target: ".env", expect: "deny", why: "exact pattern" },
    49	  { target: ".env.local", expect: "deny", why: "`.env.*` pattern" },
    50	  { target: "secrets/db.json", expect: "deny", why: "`secrets/**` prefix match" },
    51	  { target: "secrets/nested/deep.json", expect: "deny", why: "`secrets/**` at depth" },
    52	  { target: ".git", expect: "deny", why: "the prefix itself" },
    53	  // ── protected targets, absolute form: MUST agree with relative ──
    54	  { target: "<ABS>/.git/config", expect: "deny", why: "absolute path resolves to the same target" },
    55	  { target: "<ABS>/.env", expect: "deny", why: "absolute exact" },
    56	  { target: "<ABS>/secrets/x", expect: "deny", why: "absolute prefix" },
    57	  // ── not protected: must NOT be over-blocked ──
    58	  { target: "src/cli.ts", expect: "allow", why: "ordinary source file" },
    59	  { target: "README.md", expect: "allow", why: "ordinary file" },
    60	  { target: ".github/workflows/ci.yml", expect: "allow", why: "`.git/**` must not match `.github/`" },
    61	  { target: ".gitignore", expect: "allow", why: "`.git/**` must not match `.gitignore`" },
    62	  { target: ".environment", expect: "allow", why: "`.env` must not match a longer name" },
    63	  { target: "secrets.txt", expect: "allow", why: "`secrets/**` must not match a sibling file" },
    64	  { target: "src/secrets/x.ts", expect: "allow", why: "`secrets/**` is rooted, not a substring" },
    65	  // ── outside the workspace: containment, not protection ──
    66	  { target: "/etc/passwd", expect: "allow", why: "outside the workspace; containment is a different gate (router rejects it)" },
    67	];
    68

    48	    const result = await checkOwnershipGate(
    49	      { registry: reg, resolver },
    50	      "agent-1", "file.create", { path: "src/main.ts" }, true,
    51	    );
    52	
    53	    assert.notEqual(result, null);
    54	    assert.equal(result!.kind, "error");
    55	    assert.ok(result!.message.includes("Ownership conflict"));
    56	  });
    57

    62	      mode: "exclusive-write",
    63	    });

     1	/**
     2	 * runtime-gate.ts — Graph-node authorization adapter (R3.7).
     3	 *
     4	 * Graph nodes are the sequential `alix graph` path; this module keeps the
     5	 * graph-specific lifecycle (capability coverage, approval reuse/creation,
     6	 * headless carve-out) but routes every POLICY decision through the ONE
     7	 * authorization boundary, `ExecutionAuthorization` — the same service tools
     8	 * and coordination workers use. `GraphExecutor` is a sequential graph CLI
     9	 * adapter, not a second scheduling authority (scheduling belongs to
    10	 * `CoordinationScheduler`).
    11	 */
    12	import type { CardRegistry } from "../../capabilities/registry/card-registry.js";
    13	import { resolveCapabilities, type CapabilityResolution } from "../../capabilities/registry/capability-resolver.js";
    14	import type { TaskNode } from "../../coordination/kernel/task-graph.js";
    15	import type { ApprovalStore } from "../approvals/approval-store.js";
    16	import type { AuditStore } from "../audit/audit-store.js";
    17	import type { PolicyGate } from "./policy-gate.js";
    18	import type { AlixConfig } from "../../operations/config/schema.js";
    19	import { ExecutionAuthorization } from "../../runtime-state/runtime/execution-authorization.js";
    20	
    21	export type RuntimeGateStatus = "ready" | "blocked" | "needs_approval";
    22	
    23	export interface RuntimeGateDecision {
    24	  status: RuntimeGateStatus;
    25	  capabilityResolution?: CapabilityResolution;
    26	  policyDecision?: "allow" | "ask" | "deny";
    27	  policyRuleId?: string;
    28	  policyReason?: string;
    29	  approvalId?: string;
    30	  reason: string;
    31	}
    32	
    33	export interface RuntimeGateInput {
    34	  node: TaskNode;
    35	  registry: CardRegistry;
    36	  policyGate: PolicyGate;
    37	  approvalStore?: ApprovalStore;
    38	  auditStore?: AuditStore;
    39	  config: AlixConfig;
    40	  /** Graph workspace root (required for the shared authorization boundary). */
    41	  cwd?: string;
    42	  /** Optional session identity passed to the authorization boundary. */
    43	  sessionId?: string;
    44	  /** Precomputed capability coverage (GraphExecutor computes it once). */
    45	  capabilityResolution?: CapabilityResolution;
    46	  /** Injected authorization boundary; defaults to one over `policyGate`. */
    47	  authorization?: ExecutionAuthorization;
    48	}
    49	
    50	export async function evaluateRuntimeGate(input: RuntimeGateInput): Promise<RuntimeGateDecision> {
    51	  const { node, registry, approvalStore, auditStore, policyGate, config } = input;
    52	  const caps = node.requiredCapabilities ?? [];
    53	
    54	  // Layer 1: Capability coverage check
    55	  if (caps.length > 0) {
    56	    const capResult = input.capabilityResolution ?? resolveCapabilities({
    57	      requiredCapabilities: caps,
    58	      domain: node.domain,
    59	      executionProfile: (node as any).executionProfile,
    60	      registry,
    61	    });
    62	    if (capResult.missingCapabilities.length > 0) {
    63	      auditStore?.append({ action: "runtime.blocked", actor: "system", details: {
    64	        graphId: node.graphId, nodeId: node.id,
    65	        capability: caps.join(","),
    66	        reason: `Missing capabilities: ${capResult.missingCapabilities.join(", ")}`,
    67	      }}).catch(() => {});
    68	      return {
    69	        status: "blocked",
    70	        capabilityResolution: capResult,
    71	        reason: `Missing capabilities: ${capResult.missingCapabilities.join(", ")}`,
    72	      };
    73	    }
    74	    // Layer 2: Policy evaluation across all capabilities, through the ONE
    75	    // authorization boundary. Apply the most restrictive: deny > ask > allow.
    76	    const authorization = input.authorization ?? new ExecutionAuthorization({ policyGate });
    77	    let overall: { decision: "allow" | "ask" | "deny"; ruleId?: string; reason?: string; approvalId?: string } | undefined;
    78	
    79	    for (const cap of caps) {
    80	      const decision = await authorization.evaluate({
    81	        requestId: `${node.graphId ?? "?"}:${node.id}:${cap}`,
    82	        capability: cap,
    83	        cwd: input.cwd ?? "",
    84	        sessionMode: config.permissions.sessionMode ?? "ask",
    85	        sessionId: input.sessionId ?? "",
    86	        nodeId: node.id,
    87	        graphId: node.graphId,
    88	        source: "graph",
    89	      });
    90	      const ruleId = decision.policyRuleId;
    91	      if (decision.status === "denied") {
    92	        overall = { decision: "deny", ruleId, reason: decision.reason };
    93	        break;
    94	      }
    95	      if (decision.status === "approval_required" && (!overall || overall.decision === "allow")) {
    96	        // A real approval id only; the boundary substitutes "unknown" when the
    97	        // policy layer did not create one, which must fall through to the
    98	        // graph-specific reuse/create path below.
    99	        overall = {
   100	          decision: "ask",
   101	          ruleId,
   102	          reason: decision.reason,
   103	          approvalId: decision.approvalId !== "unknown" ? decision.approvalId : undefined,
   104	        };
   105	      }
   106	      if (decision.status === "allowed" && !overall) {
   107	        overall = { decision: "allow", ruleId };
   108	      }
   109	    }
   110	
   111	    if (overall?.decision === "deny") {
   112	      auditStore?.append({ action: "policy.denied", actor: "policy", details: {
   113	        graphId: node.graphId, nodeId: node.id,
   114	        capability: caps.join(","), policyRuleId: overall.ruleId,
   115	        policyDecision: "deny", reason: overall.reason,
   116	      }}).catch(() => {});
   117	      return {
   118	        status: "blocked",
   119	        capabilityResolution: capResult,
   120	        policyDecision: "deny",
   121	        policyRuleId: overall.ruleId,
   122	        policyReason: overall.reason,
   123	        reason: overall.reason ?? `Blocked by policy rule: ${overall.ruleId}`,
   124	      };
   125	    }
   126	
   127	    if (overall?.decision === "ask") {
   128	      // When PolicyGate already handled the approval lifecycle, use it directly
   129	      if (overall.approvalId) {
   130	        auditStore?.append({ action: "policy.asked", actor: "policy", details: {
   131	          graphId: node.graphId, nodeId: node.id,
   132	          capability: caps.join(","), approvalId: overall.approvalId,
   133	          policyDecision: "ask", reason: overall.reason,
   134	        }}).catch(() => {});
   135	        return {
   136	          status: "needs_approval",
   137	          capabilityResolution: capResult,
   138	          policyDecision: "ask",
   139	          policyRuleId: overall.ruleId,
   140	          policyReason: overall.reason,
   141	          approvalId: overall.approvalId,
   142	          reason: `Pending approval: ${overall.approvalId}`,
   143	        };
   144	      }
   145	
   146	      if (!approvalStore) {
   147	        // Headless mirror of the policy-gate carve-out: graph nodes needing
   148	        // only headless-safe capabilities (read-only public web, plus the
   149	        // zero-side-effect task.complete signal) stay runnable without a
   150	        // store; anything else still fails closed.
nl: write error: Broken pipe

   384	
   385	  /** End a tool span exactly once; tracing failures never change tool results. */
   386	  private endToolSpan(client: TraceClient, span: TraceSpan, outcome: SpanOutcome): void {
   387	    try {
   388	      client.endSpan(span, outcome);
   389	    } catch {
   390	      // Tracing must never throw into tool execution.
   391	    }
   392	  }
   393	
   394	  private async dispatch(request: ToolCallRequest): Promise<ExecuteResult> {
   395	    const startedAt = Date.now();
   396	    const { toolCallId, name } = request;
   397	    const agentPayload = request.agentId ? { agentId: request.agentId } : {};
   398	    let args = request.args;
   399	    const capability = inferCapability(name);
   400	    const canonicalCapability = canonicalCapabilityOf(name);
   401	    // Routers need the same owned-path authority policy uses: a write worker
   402	    // rewriting its own declared output must be able to replace it.
   403	    if (!request.ownedPaths?.length && this.ownedPaths?.length) {
   404	      (request as { ownedPaths?: readonly string[] }).ownedPaths = this.ownedPaths;
   405	    }
   406	
   407	    // === TOOL REPAIR LAYER — runs before policy so hash and decision use repaired args ===
   408	    let repairHint: string | undefined;
   409	    if (this.repair && name !== "task.complete" && !name.startsWith("mcp.")) {
   410	      const repairResult = this.repair.process(name, args);
   411	      if (repairResult.repaired) {
   412	        repairHint = repairResult.hint;
   413	        args = repairResult.args;
   414	        (request as Record<string, unknown>).args = args;
   415	      }
   416	    }
   417	    // === END TOOL REPAIR ===
   418	
   419	    const argumentHash = hashArgs(args);
   420	    // Track the claim across execution so the crash-recovery state is honest:
   421	    // a claimed approval that never reports an outcome stays `pending` and a
   422	    // later replay classifies it `unverified` instead of reissuing authority.
   423	    let claimedApprovalId: string | undefined;
   424	    // T5 hierarchy: executionId → invocationId → toolCallId — typed via CorrelationContext, no loose Record
   425	    // For T5 parallel path, correlation is required (asserted in tool-correlation); for serial/back-compat allow fallback to empty.
   426	    const correlation: CorrelationContext = {
   427	      executionId: request.executionId ?? "",
   428	      invocationId: request.invocationId ?? "",
   429	    };
   430	
   431	    await this.logEvent(TOOL_EVENT_TYPES.REQUESTED, {
   432	      toolCallId,
   433	      toolName: name,
   434	      capability,
   435	      canonicalCapability,
   436	      argumentHash,
   437	      argsPreview: sanitizeArgs(args),
   438	      executionId: correlation.executionId,
   439	      invocationId: correlation.invocationId,
   440	      ...agentPayload,
   441	      ...(request.replayId ? { replayId: request.replayId } : {}),
   442	    });
   443	
   444	    if (request.allowedMutationPaths?.length) {
   445	      const mutationPaths = extractMutationPaths(name, args);
   446	      const allowed = new Set(request.allowedMutationPaths.map((path) => resolve(this.root, path)));
   447	      const changedTarget = mutationPaths.find((path) => !allowed.has(resolve(this.root, path)));
   448	      if (changedTarget) {
   449	        const message = `Task constraint denied: ${changedTarget} is not the explicitly requested target`;
   450	        await this.logEvent(TOOL_EVENT_TYPES.FAILED, {
   451	          toolCallId,
   452	          toolName: name,
   453	          error: message,
   454	          durationMs: 0,
   455	          canonicalCapability,
   456	          argumentHash,
   457	          executionId: correlation.executionId,
   458	          invocationId: correlation.invocationId,
   459	          ...agentPayload,
   460	          ...(request.replayId ? { replayId: request.replayId } : {}),
   461	        });
   462	        return { kind: "error", message, retryable: false };
   463	      }
   464	    }
   465	
   466	    // Continuation resumes bypass main policy gate — approval was already verified
   467	    // by ContinuationManager. R1.5: the executor independently re-validates the
   468	    // durable approval (present, still `approved`, not expired) — a caller-set
   469	    // `source` string is not authorization. Missing/invalid evidence fails closed.
   470	    if (request.source === "continuation-resume") {
   471	      const approval = request.approvalId && this.approvalStore
   472	        ? this.approvalStore.get?.(request.approvalId)
   473	        : undefined;
   474	      const stillAuthorized =
   475	        !!approval &&
   476	        approval.status === "approved" &&
   477	        (!approval.expiresAt || new Date(approval.expiresAt).getTime() > Date.now());
   478	      if (!stillAuthorized) {
   479	        return {
   480	          kind: "denied",
   481	          reason: request.approvalId
   482	            ? `Continuation resume rejected: approval ${request.approvalId} missing, not approved, or expired`
   483	            : "Continuation resume rejected: no durable approval backing this request",
   484	        };
   485	      }
   486	      const contGateResult = await this.checkOwnershipGate(request, name, args);
   487	      if (contGateResult) return contGateResult;
   488	      // R1.5: the continuation tombstone is NOT authorization — claim the
   489	      // backing approval atomically so two parallel resumes cannot both run.
   490	      if (request.approvalId) {
   491	        const claimed = await this.claimApproval(request.approvalId, {
   492	          ...this.invocationIdentity(capability, name, argumentHash, request.agentId),
   493	          });
   494	        if (claimed) return claimed;
   495	        claimedApprovalId = request.approvalId;
   496	      }
   497	      await this.logEvent(TOOL_EVENT_TYPES.STARTED, {
   498	        toolCallId,
   499	        toolName: name,
   500	        argumentHash,
   501	        executionId: correlation.executionId,
   502	        invocationId: correlation.invocationId,
   503	        ...agentPayload,
   504	        ...(request.replayId ? { replayId: request.replayId } : {}),
   505	      });
   506	      const resumed = await this.toolAwareRouter.downstream.execute(request);
   507	      if (claimedApprovalId) {
   508	        await this.recordApprovalOutcome(claimedApprovalId, resumed.kind === "success" ? "succeeded" : "failed");
   509	      }
   510	      return resumed;
   511	    }
   512	
   513	    // Safe shell bypass for ask mode: read-only commands skip policy gate
   514	    const mode = this.config.permissions.sessionMode ?? "ask";
   515	    if (mode === "ask" && name === "shell.run") {
   516	      const command = typeof args?.command === "string" ? args.command : "";
   517	      if (command && isSafeShellCommand(command)) {
   518	        // Skip the approval gate for a whitelisted read-only command, but do
   519	        // not execute it here: the downstream ShellToolRouter owns workspace
   520	        // path validation and command-failure classification.
   521	        await this.logEvent(TOOL_EVENT_TYPES.STARTED, {
   522	          toolCallId,
   523	          toolName: name,
   524	          argumentHash,
   525	          executionId: correlation.executionId,
   526	          invocationId: correlation.invocationId,
   527	          ...agentPayload,
   528	          ...(request.replayId ? { replayId: request.replayId } : {}),
   529	        });
   530	        return await this.toolAwareRouter.downstream.execute(request);
   531	      }
   532	    }
   533	
   534	    // Unified authorization via ExecutionAuthorization — composes PolicyGate,
   535	    // OwnershipGate, and audit/event emission into a single decision.
   536	    const { ExecutionAuthorization } = await import("../../runtime-state/runtime/execution-authorization.js");
   537	    const policyGate = await this.policyGate();
   538	    const execAuth = new ExecutionAuthorization({
   539	      policyGate,
   540	      toolRegistry: this.toolRegistry,
   541	      ownershipGateConfig: this.ownershipRegistry && this.workspacePathResolver
   542	        ? { registry: this.ownershipRegistry, resolver: this.workspacePathResolver, autoAcquire: this.config.ownership?.autoAcquire ?? true }
   543	        : undefined,
   544	    });
   545	
   546	    const decision = await execAuth.evaluate({
   547	      requestId: toolCallId,
   548	      capability,
   549	      toolName: name,
   550	      args,
   551	      cwd: this.root,
   552	      sessionMode: this.config.permissions.sessionMode ?? "ask",
   553	      sessionId: this.sessionId(),
   554	      agentId: request.agentId ?? "alix",
   555	      source: "tool",
   556	      ownedPaths: this.ownedPaths,
   557	    });
   558	
   559	    // Denied — policy or ownership rejected this call
   560	    if (decision.status === "denied") {
   561	      await this.logEvent(TOOL_EVENT_TYPES.FAILED, {
   562	        toolCallId,
   563	        toolName: name,
   564	        error: decision.reason,
   565	        durationMs: 0,
   566	        canonicalCapability,
   567	        argumentHash,
   568	        executionId: correlation.executionId,
   569	        invocationId: correlation.invocationId,
   570	        ...agentPayload,
    30	  matchedRuleId?: string;
    31	  approvalId?: string;
    32	  policyRevision?: string;
    33	  /** Exact binding key the approval was created/bound with (coordination path). */
    34	  bindingKey?: string;
    35	  /** The request fingerprint that key was computed from. */
    36	  requestFingerprint?: string;
    37	  /**
    38	   * Set only by the coordination exact-binding branch, which consumes the
    39	   * approval inside the gate. The executor must NOT claim it again.
    40	   */
    41	  approvalConsumed?: boolean;
    42	};
    43	
    44	export type ToolPolicyRequest = {
    45	  requestId: string;
    46	  toolName: string;
    47	  capability?: string;       // caller-provided; falls back to inferCapability
    48	  args: Record<string, unknown>;
    49	  cwd: string;
    50	  sessionMode: SessionMode;
    51	  sessionId?: string;
    52	  agentId?: string;
    53	  source: "tool" | "graph" | "daemon" | "tui" | "replay";
    54	  // Coordination context
    55	  coordinationRunId?: string;
    56	  workerId?: string;
    57	  workerAttempt?: number;
    58	  ownershipClaims?: WorkerOwnershipClaim[];
    59	  requestFingerprint?: string;
    60	  // Owned-path auto-approval (headless write subagents): paths this caller
    61	  // is authorized to mutate without approval.
    62	  ownedPaths?: string[];
    63	};
    64	
    65	export type CapabilityPolicyRequest = {
    66	  requestId: string;
    67	  capability: string;
    68	  sessionMode: SessionMode;
    69	  nodeId?: string;
    70	  graphId?: string;
    71	  sessionId?: string;
    72	  agentId?: string;
    73	  source: "tool" | "graph" | "daemon" | "tui" | "replay";
    74	  metadata?: Record<string, unknown>;
    75	  // Coordination context
    76	  coordinationRunId?: string;
    77	  workerId?: string;
    78	  workerAttempt?: number;
    79	  ownershipClaims?: WorkerOwnershipClaim[];
    80	  requestFingerprint?: string;
    81	};
    82	
    83	// ─── Path resolution helper ──────────────────────────────────────────
    84	
    85	/** Normalize a path argument against cwd for policy checks. */
    86	export function resolvePolicyPath(cwd: string, path: string): string {
    87	  if (path.startsWith("/")) return path;
    88	  return resolve(cwd, path);
    89	}
    90	
    91	// ─── Owned-path auto-approval helpers ─────────────────────────────────
    92	
    93	/** Write tools whose mutation targets can be scoped to ownedPaths. */
    94	const OWNED_WRITE_TOOLS = new Set(["file.create", "file.delete", "patch.apply"]);
    95	
    96	/**
    97	 * True when the request declares an owned scope for a write tool — the case
    98	 * where the owned-path rule (step 5.5) is the authority for the write.
    99	 */
   100	function hasOwnedWriteScope(request: ToolPolicyRequest): boolean {
   101	  return Boolean(request.ownedPaths?.length) && OWNED_WRITE_TOOLS.has(request.toolName);
   102	}
   103	
   104	/** Extract the mutation targets from a write-tool's args. */
   105	function mutationTargets(args: Record<string, unknown>): string[] {
   106	  const path = typeof args.path === "string" ? args.path : undefined;
   107	  if (path) return [path];
   108	  const format = typeof args.format === "string" ? args.format : undefined;
   109	  return extractPatchPaths(format, args.patchText);
   110	}
   111	
   112	// Owned-scope matching is `isWithinOwnedScope` (see the Local Contracts in
   113	// src/governance/policy/AGENTS.md). This gate runs BEFORE the file router, so a
   114	// normalization difference between them would silently deny every owned write
   115	// the router allowed — call the shared function directly, do not alias it.
     1	/**
     2	 * ownership-gate.ts — Ownership enforcement for tool execution.
     3	 *
     4	 * Sits AFTER PolicyGate, before workspace/path validation and execution.
     5	 * Delegates all authorization to registry.authorizeMutation() which operates
     6	 * under the ownership lock to reload state, check conflicts, and verify/acquire
     7	 * coverage atomically.
     8	 *
     9	 * Execution order:
    10	 *   argument repair
    11	 *   → request logging
    12	 *   → PolicyGate (skip on continuation-resume)
    13	 *   → OwnershipGate (ALWAYS — even for continuation-resume)
    14	 *   → workspace/path validation
    15	 *   → tool execution
    16	 */
    17	
    18	import type { OwnershipRegistry } from "./ownership-registry.js";
    19	import type { WorkspacePathResolver } from "../../runtime-state/runtime/workspace-path.js";
    20	import type { ToolResult } from "../../capabilities/tools/types.js";
    21	import { extractMutationTargets } from "./mutation-targets.js";
    22	
    23	export type OwnershipGateConfig = {
    24	  registry: OwnershipRegistry;
    25	  resolver: WorkspacePathResolver;
    26	  /**
    27	   * When true, automatically acquires a lease for each confident
    28	   * mutation target as the tool runs.
    29	   * Single-agent default: true. Parallel-agent: false.
    30	   */
    31	  autoAcquire?: boolean;
    32	};
    33	
    34	/**
    35	 * Check ownership for a tool call.
    36	 *
    37	 * Returns null if allowed, or a ToolResult error if blocked.
    38	 *
    39	 * Fail-closed:
    40	 * - unknown-write classification (e.g. shell.run) is denied
    41	 * - autoAcquire=false requires existing exclusive-write coverage
    42	 */
    43	export async function checkOwnershipGate(
    44	  config: OwnershipGateConfig,
    45	  agentId: string,
    46	  toolName: string,
    47	  args: Record<string, unknown>,
    48	  mutates: boolean,
    49	): Promise<ToolResult | null> {
    50	  // Non-mutating tools pass without ownership check
    51	  if (!mutates) return null;
    52	
    53	  // Extract mutation targets with classification
    54	  const extraction = extractMutationTargets(toolName, args, config.resolver);
    55	
    56	  // Fail-closed: unknown-write (shell, patch without target, etc.)
    57	  if (extraction.classification === "unknown-write") {
    58	    return {
    59	      kind: "error",
    60	      message: `Cannot determine mutation targets for ${toolName} — ownership check failed closed. ` +
    61	        `Acquire an explicit lease covering the expected paths and retry.`,
    62	      retryable: false,
    63	    };
    64	  }
    65	
    66	  // no-write tools (read-only shell etc.) pass through
    67	  if (extraction.classification === "no-write") {
    68	    return null;
    69	  }
    70	
    71	  // Delegate to registry which acquires lock, reloads, checks, and persists
    72	  const decision = await config.registry.authorizeMutation({
    73	    agentId,
    74	    targets: extraction.targets,
    75	    autoAcquire: config.autoAcquire !== false,
    76	  });
    77	
    78	  if (!decision.allowed) {
    79	    return {
    80	      kind: "error",
    81	      message: `Ownership check failed: ${decision.reason}`,
    82	      retryable: false,
    83	    };
    84	  }
    85	
    86	  return null; // pass
    87	}

    52	function sanitizeArgs(args: Record<string, unknown>): Record<string, unknown> {
    53	  const sensitive = ["password", "token", "secret", "key"];
    54	  return Object.fromEntries(
    55	    Object.entries(args).map(([k, v]) => [
    56	      k,
    57	      sensitive.some((s) => k.toLowerCase().includes(s)) ? "[REDACTED]" : v,
    58	    ])

    62	export type ExecuteResult =
    63	  | ToolResult
    64	  | {
    65	      kind: "denied";
    66	      reason: string;
    67	      /**
    68	       * Set on the approval-gated FIRST attempt of `handleToolCall`'s
    69	       * execute-twice flow — the pause that waits for operator resolution and
    70	       * is then re-executed for real. Structurally flagged (design §21); the
    71	       * `reason` string keeps its historical "Approval required (id): …" shape.
    72	       */
    73	      approvalRequired?: true;
    74	      /** The pending approval's id, when `approvalRequired` is set. */
    75	      approvalId?: string;
    76	    };
    77	
    78	/**
    79	 * True when a `denied` result is the approval-gated FIRST attempt in
    80	 * `handleToolCall`'s execute-twice flow. That attempt does not execute the
    81	 * tool — it waits for operator approval and is re-executed for real, so it
    82	 * must NOT emit a terminal span (design §21 exactly-once-per-physical-call).
    83	 * Genuine policy/ownership/mcp denials are NOT this case and keep their single
    84	 * error span.
    85	 *
    86	 * The structured `approvalRequired` flag is the primary signal. The reason
    87	 * prefix fallback keeps hand-constructed denials (tests, tool adapters that
    88	 * build `{ kind: "denied", reason }` directly) classified correctly without
    89	 * requiring them to know the flag.
    90	 */
    91	function isPreApprovalDenial(result: ExecuteResult | null | undefined): boolean {
    92	  return (
    93	    result?.kind === "denied" &&
    94	    (result.approvalRequired === true || /^Approval required \(/.test(result.reason))
    95	  );
    96	}

   167	   * call — without this, the loop sees "Approval required" as a generic
   168	   * "Access denied" and the model just retries, generating one new

   171	  getApproval(id: string): { status?: string } | undefined {

   175	  /**
   176	   * Authoritative approval read for a call that is WAITING on a decision.
   177	   *
   178	   * The in-memory `get` above reflects only decisions this process made. In a
   179	   * coordination run the operator resolves from the TUI while the worker is a
   180	   * separate execution context, so a wait built solely on `get` would never
   181	   * observe the decision — the worker would sit until its 5-minute timeout and
   182	   * then treat a granted approval as expired.
   183	   *

   185	   * record, `undefined` when the record is gone, and a throw on a transient read
   186	   * error which this maps to `null` so the caller can keep waiting instead of
   187	   * mistaking a broken read for a denial.

   189	  async loadApprovalForWait(id: string): Promise<{ status?: string } | undefined | null> {
   190	    if (!this.approvalStore?.loadFresh) return this.getApproval(id);
    65	  signal?: AbortSignal,
    66	  envAllowlist?: string[],
    67	): { promise: Promise<ToolResult>; cancel: () => void } {
    68	  const child = spawn(command, [], {
    69	    cwd: cwd || undefined,
    70	    shell: true,
    71	    detached: process.platform !== "win32",
    72	    env: buildChildEnv(envAllowlist),
    73	  });
    74	  let stdout = "";
    75	  let stderr = "";
    76	  let settled = false;
    77	
    78	  const promise = new Promise<ToolResult>((resolve, reject) => {
    79	    const onAbort = (): void => {
    80	      if (settled) return;
    81	      settled = true;
    82	      if (signal) signal.removeEventListener("abort", onAbort);
    83	      // Kill the child (the operator-abort kill path) and route the outcome
    84	      // to cancellation, never to a "command exited with code" failure.
    85	      killProcessTree(child.pid);
    86	      reject(new ExecutionCancelledError(signalReason(signal) ?? "cancelled by operator"));
    87	    };
    88	    const finish = (result: ToolResult) => {
    89	      if (settled) return;
    90	      settled = true;
    91	      if (signal) signal.removeEventListener("abort", onAbort);
    92	      resolve(result);
    93	    };
    94	
    95	    if (signal) {
    96	      // Already-aborted at spawn time: kill the just-spawned child and reject
    97	      // immediately. Otherwise listen — the loop may cancel mid-flight.
    98	      if (signal.aborted) onAbort();
    99	      else signal.addEventListener("abort", onAbort, { once: true });
   100	    }
   101	
   102	    child.stdout.on("data", (chunk) => { stdout += chunk; });
   103	    child.stderr.on("data", (chunk) => { stderr += chunk; });
   104	
   105	    child.on("close", (code) => {
   106	      if (settled) return;
   107	      const combined = stderr ? `${stdout}\n--- stderr ---\n${stderr}` : stdout;
   108	      const output = truncate(combined, MAX_BYTES);
   109	      if ((code ?? 0) !== 0) {
   110	        finish({ kind: "error", message: `Command exited with code ${code}: ${command}\n${output}` });
   111	        return;
   112	      }
   113	      finish({ kind: "success", output, exitCode: code ?? 0 });
   114	    });
   115	
   116	    child.on("error", (err) => {
   117	      finish({ kind: "error", message: `Command failed: ${command} -- ${err.message}` });
   118	    });
   119	  });
   120	
   121	  return {
   122	    promise,
   123	    cancel: () => {
   124	      if (!settled) killProcessTree(child.pid);
   125	    },
   126	  };
   127	}
   128	
   129	function killProcessTree(pid: number | undefined): void {
   130	  if (!pid) return;
   131	  if (process.platform === "win32") {
   132	    // Synchronous: `cancel()` is fire-and-forget and the caller returns the
   133	    // timeout/abort result immediately, so the kill MUST complete before we
   134	    // return — otherwise the child (and any grandchild) outlives the call,
   135	    // leaving the test's temp dir locked (EBUSY) and the "child was killed"
   136	    // assertions racing. `taskkill /T /F` is fast.
   137	    try {
   138	      execFileSync("taskkill", ["/pid", String(pid), "/T", "/F"], { stdio: "ignore", timeout: 5_000 });
   139	    } catch { /* already exited / taskkill unavailable */ }
   140	    return;
   141	  }
   142	  try { process.kill(-pid, "SIGKILL"); } catch {
   143	    try { process.kill(pid, "SIGKILL"); } catch { /* already exited */ }
   144	  }
   145	}

   489	   * constructed with an EventLog (`ApprovalStore.emit()` is a no-op otherwise).
   490	   * This gate must therefore emit the creation record itself on exactly the
   491	   * paths where the store cannot, or the approval lifecycle disappears from the
   492	   * event stream — headless `alix run` is one such composition root.
   493	   *
   494	   * Fail toward "the store emits": a late/absent capability must never cause a
   495	   * second creation event, because duplicate records inflate approval-volume
   496	   * counters (F1-B). Only a store that positively reports it has no EventLog
   497	   * moves the emission here.
   498	   */
   499	  private storeEmitsCreationEvents(): boolean {
   500	    const store = this.deps.approvalStore as
   501	      | { emitsLifecycleEvents?: boolean }
   502	      | undefined;
   503	    return store?.emitsLifecycleEvents !== false;
   504	  }
   505	
   506	  /**
   507	   * Evaluate a capability against policy (for graph node checks).
   508	   * Simpler than evaluateToolCall — no path/command/evasion checks.
   509	   */
   510	  async evaluateCapability(request: CapabilityPolicyRequest): Promise<PolicyGateDecision> {
   511	    const policyRevision = computePolicyRevision(this.config);
   512	
   513	    const toolDecision = this.config.permissions.tools?.[request.capability];
   514	
   515	    if (toolDecision) {
   516	      const effective = applySessionMode(toolDecision, request.sessionMode);
   517	      if (effective === "allow") {
   518	        return { requestId: request.requestId, capability: request.capability, decision: "allow", reason: `Allowed by tool policy (mode: ${request.sessionMode})`, matchedRuleId: `tool-policy-${request.capability}`, policyRevision };
   519	      }
   520	      if (effective === "deny") {
   521	        return { requestId: request.requestId, capability: request.capability, decision: "deny", reason: `Denied by tool policy (mode: ${request.sessionMode})`, matchedRuleId: `tool-policy-${request.capability}`, policyRevision };
   522	      }
   523	    }
   524	
   525	    const defaultDecision = applySessionMode(this.config.permissions.default ?? "ask", request.sessionMode);

   334	  /** Validate a path through the resolver. Returns error result if blocked. */
   335	  private checkPath(rawPath: string): ToolResult | null {
   336	    const r = this.pathResolver.check(rawPath);
   337	    if (!r.insideWorkspace || !this.pathResolver.isCanonicalInWorkspace(r.absolute)) {
   338	      return { kind: "error", message: "Shell access denied: path is outside workspace (" + r.absolute + ")", retryable: false };
   339	    }
   340	    // Check protected first — user-configured protections take priority over
   341	    // the generic sensitive pattern message for better UX (mirrors FileToolRouter).
   342	    if (r.protected && r.insideWorkspace) {
   343	      return { kind: "error", message: "Shell access denied: path is protected (" + r.absolute + ")" };
   344	    }
   345	    if (r.sensitive) {
   346	      return { kind: "error", message: "Shell access denied: path is sensitive (" + r.absolute + ")" };
   347	    }
   348	    return null;
   349	  }

    10	function makeConfig(overrides?: Partial<AlixConfig>): AlixConfig {
    11	  const base: AlixConfig = {
    12	    version: 1,
    13	    model: { provider: "mock", name: "mock", streaming: false, maxIterations: 10, maxContextTokens: 32000 },
    14	    permissions: {
    15	      sessionMode: "ask",
    16	      default: "ask",
    17	      tools: {},
    18	      protectedPaths: ["/etc/**", "/home/*/.ssh/**"],
    19	      allowNetworkDomains: [],
    20	      denyCommands: ["rm -rf /", "shutdown"],
    21	    },
    22	    context: { repoMap: false, repoMapMode: "lite", maxRepoMapTokens: 0, semanticSearch: false, includeGitStatus: false, pinnedFiles: [] },
    23	    runtime: { provider: "process", shell: "/bin/bash", commandTimeoutMs: 10000, envAllowlist: [] },
    24	    ui: { enabled: false, host: "", port: 0, transport: "sse" as const },
    25	  };
    26	  if (!overrides) return base;
    27	
    28	  // Merge top-level fields
    29	  const merged = { ...base, ...overrides } as any;
    30	  // Deep-merge permissions so overrides don't wipe the full object
    31	  if (overrides.permissions) {
    32	    merged.permissions = { ...base.permissions, ...overrides.permissions as any };
    33	  }
    34	  return merged as AlixConfig;
    35	}

    14	};
    15	
    16	// Policies keyed by role or style
    17	const READ_ONLY_ROLES: SubagentRole[] = AGENT_REGISTRY
    18	  .filter((a) => getPolicyBucket(a.role) === "read")
    19	  .map((a) => a.role);
    20	
    21	const WRITE_ROLES: SubagentRole[] = AGENT_REGISTRY
    22	  .filter((a) => getPolicyBucket(a.role) === "write")
    23	  .map((a) => a.role);
    24	
    25	const RESEARCH_ROLES: SubagentRole[] = AGENT_REGISTRY
    26	  .filter((a) => getPolicyBucket(a.role) === "research")
    27	  .map((a) => a.role);
    28	
    29	export function getToolPolicy(role: SubagentRole): ToolPolicy {
    30	  if (READ_ONLY_ROLES.includes(role)) {
    31	    return {
    32	      allowedCategories: ["read"],
    33	      maxIterations: 5,
    34	      maxShellCommandsPerIteration: 3,
    35	      allowMcpTools: false,
    36	    };
    37	  }
    38	  if (WRITE_ROLES.includes(role)) {
    39	    return {
    40	      allowedCategories: ["read", "write", "mcp"],
    41	      maxIterations: 5,
    42	      maxShellCommandsPerIteration: 5,
    43	      allowMcpTools: true,
    44	    };
    45	  }
    46	  if (RESEARCH_ROLES.includes(role)) {
    47	    return {
    48	      allowedCategories: ["read", "mcp"],
    49	      maxIterations: 5,
    50	      maxShellCommandsPerIteration: 3,
    51	      allowMcpTools: true,
    52	    };
    53	  }
    54	  // Default: read-only fallback
    55	  return {
    56	    allowedCategories: ["read"],
    57	    maxIterations: 3,
    58	    maxShellCommandsPerIteration: 2,
    59	    allowMcpTools: false,
    60	  };
    61	}
    62	
    63	// Built-in tools that are not workspace writers. Deliberately NOT called
    64	// "read-only": the set includes `alix_shell_run` (arbitrary command execution,
    65	// separately `ask`-gated in DEFAULT_CONFIG.permissions.tools) and the
    66	// coordination/state readers, so "non-write" is the honest description of what
    67	// these have in common. Membership decides which `allowedCategories` a role
    68	// needs, not what the tool may do.

   101	  return tools.filter((tool) => {
   102	    // done is always allowed

   106

   108	    if (tool.name.startsWith("mcp__")) {
   109	      if (!policy.allowMcpTools) return false;
   110	      return true;

   455	export class PatchToolRouter implements ToolRouter {
   456	  constructor(
   457	    private readonly root: string,
   458	    private readonly config: AlixConfig,
   459	    private editFormatPolicy?: EditFormatPolicy,
   460	    private checkpointManager?: CheckpointManager,
   115	  private persistIfChanged(): void {
   116	    if (!this.changed) return;
   117	    this.revision++;
   118	    const store: OwnershipStore = { version: STORE_VERSION, revision: this.revision, records: this.records };
   119	    mkdirSync(dirname(this.storagePath), { recursive: true });
   120	    const tmp = this.storagePath + ".tmp";
   121	    writeFileSync(tmp, JSON.stringify(store, null, 2), "utf-8");
   122	    renameSync(tmp, this.storagePath);
   123	  }
   124	
   125	  /**
   126	   * Execute fn inside lock. On failure: clear pending events, reload,
   127	   * rethrow. Events emitted after lock release.
   128	   */
   129	  private async withLock<T>(fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T | null> {
   130	    const acquired = await this.lock.acquire();
   131	    if (!acquired) {
   132	      await this.emitEvents([{ event: "ownership.lock_failed", data: { reason: "timeout" } }]);
   133	      return null;
   134	    }
   135	    let events: Array<{ event: string; data: Record<string, unknown> }> = [];
   136	    try {
   137	      this.reloadFromDisk();
   138	      this.applyExpiration();
   139	      this.changed = false;
   140	      const result = await fn();
   141	      this.persistIfChanged();
   142	      events = this.takePendingEvents();
   143	      return result;
   144	    } catch (err) {
   145	      this.pendingEvents = [];
   146	      this.reloadFromDisk();
   147	      throw err;
   148	    } finally {
   149	      this.lock.release();
   150	      await this.emitEvents(events);
   151	    }
   152	  }
   153	
   154	  private takePendingEvents(): Array<{ event: string; data: Record<string, unknown> }> {
   155	    const batch = this.pendingEvents;
   156	    this.pendingEvents = [];
   157	    return batch;
   158	  }
   159	
   160	  private async emitEvents(
   161	    batch: Array<{ event: string; data: Record<string, unknown> }>,
   162	  ): Promise<void> {
   163	    for (const { event, data } of batch) {
   164	      await this.emitEvent(event, data);
   165	    }
   166	  }
   167	
   168	  private markChanged(): void { this.changed = true; }
   169	
   170	  // ═══ Query (refreshes from disk; no mutation lock) ═══════════
   171	
   172	  /** Reload state from disk. For read operations that need fresh data. */
   173	  async refresh(): Promise<void> {
   174	    const acquired = await this.lock.acquire(2000);
   175	    if (!acquired) return;

CLAUSE VERDICTS

Review target: HEAD 19999d03cc3e5e59a7dffda2d09bffefc421c33e. Record pins ea863a25b5dd0e014d275bc1c4b979b7b7d5afee. Reviewed source/test paths showed no committed differences between those commits. GitNexus index was 41 commits stale; source supplied final evidence.

All cited test files exist. Ran 123 focused, filesystem-pure cases from existing compiled tests: command-classifier 7, rule-evaluator 13, path-scope 98, selected policy-gate 5; all passed. Did not rebuild or run fixtures that write files, execute mutation commands, or initialize durable stores. Therefore the record’s 282/282 execution claim remains independently unverified.

- F8b — PARTIAL. Facade omits decision methods; pending consumption is rejected and status remains pending: tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts:82, :90. Cross-worker outcome rejection has meaningful assertions at :195. Constructor guard exists at src/governance/approvals/worker-approval-port.ts:180, but its supposed negative test never triggers it. Neither process confinement nor exclusive access through this facade is established.

- Authority — NOT QUALIFIED. Gate-level mode tests meaningfully assert deny: tests/policy/policy-gate.test.ts:113. Runtime dispatch bypasses that gate for safe-shell commands in ask mode: src/capabilities/tools/executor.ts:513. Deny-wins for overlapping rules is also absent.

- Crash/recovery — PARTIAL. Ordinary replay tests assert denial, zero effects, and consumed/unverified state: tests/security/approval-crash-recovery.test.ts:95. However, coordination replay does not meet that contract, and stale-lock recovery admits a two-claimant interleaving.

- Ownership — NOT QUALIFIED for “every declared-scope write.” File-tool fix is real and meaningfully pinned: tests/policy/policy-gate.test.ts:429. Shell execution can bypass declared owned paths. Router backstop coverage also sits outside the cited evidence set.

- Denied-action negatives — PARTIAL. Decision assertions exist, but the four cited files do not demonstrate dispatch suppression plus absence of prohibited effects. Classification is repeatedly presented as execution evidence.


DEFECTS

D1 — major — Hard deny bypassed by safe-shell dispatch.
src/capabilities/tools/executor.ts:513 dispatches directly to downstream at :530, before authorization at :534.
Counterexample: ask mode, shell.run: deny, denyCommands: ["ls"], command "ls". A probe executing the extracted source dispatch body reached a mock router once; policy gate calls: zero.
Minimal fix: evaluate mandatory denies before bypassing approval prompts. Add executor-level denied-result and zero-router-call assertions.

D2 — major — Declared ownership does not constrain shell writes.
src/governance/policy/policy-gate.ts:94 limits owned-scope enforcement to file.create, file.delete, patch.apply. Capability allow returns at :331 for shell.run. ShellToolRouter executes arbitrary commands at src/capabilities/tools/tool-router.ts:451 without checking ownedPaths.
A gate probe with ownedPaths ["inside.txt"], shell.run allowed, and "printf x > outside.txt" returned allow. Child executor construction supplies ownedPaths but no ownership registry: src/agents/subagent-cli.ts:723.
Minimal fix: deny unscopable mutation channels for scoped workers, or enforce scope through an actual execution sandbox. Cover shell and dynamic tools explicitly.

D3 — major — Stale-lock takeover can remove a new owner’s live lock.
src/governance/approvals/approval-store-lock.ts:48 checks staleness, then unconditionally removes the directory at :49.
Two processes can observe one stale lock; the delayed contender then deletes the first contender’s replacement lock. Both can load the approved state and claim.
The ledger CAS does not necessarily prevent this: src/governance/approvals/approval-store.ts:308 obtains the latest version during commit, rather than retaining the version read with the approval.
Minimal fix: atomic conditional approved-to-consumed transition against the loaded version, plus race-safe stale-lock recovery. Add deterministic two-process contention coverage.
This is a source-derived interleaving, not an executed race reproduction.

D4 — major — Coordination replay creates replacement authority instead of denying uncertainty.
src/governance/policy/policy-gate.ts:715 recognizes only an approved exact binding. A consumed binding falls through to creation of another pending approval at :739. It is neither classified unverified nor denied.
Minimal fix: explicitly handle consumed coordination bindings before creating requests; assert deny, original unverified state, and no replacement request.

D5 — major — Preconsumed approvals lose execution receipts.
src/capabilities/tools/executor.ts:682 skips both claiming and assigning claimedApprovalId when approvalConsumed is true. Outcome recording at :875 requires that variable.
Consequently, gate-consumed coordination approvals can remain pending after successful execution.
Minimal fix: retain the approval ID for both consumption routes; test through executor, effect, terminal receipt, and replay.

D6 — major — Worker facade is not an exclusive approval-authority boundary.
Normal wiring uses openWorkerApprovalPort: src/agents/subagent-cli.ts:708. But that function itself imports unrestricted ApprovalStore at src/governance/approvals/worker-approval-port.ts:119.
Child spawning provides ordinary process execution under the same user, without an OS authority boundary: src/agents/subagent-manager.ts:364. Approval CLI directly calls store.resolve without worker-origin checks: src/interfaces/cli/commands/approval.ts:106.
A child capable of executing JavaScript or invoking that CLI can reopen the store. The facade narrows an object; it does not confine a compromised process.
Minimal fix: explicitly limit the claim’s threat model, or move decision authority behind parent-owned IPC/storage permissions inaccessible to children.

D7 — major — Worker identity is not enforced across the facade’s mutation surface.
src/governance/approvals/worker-approval-port.ts:162 forwards consumeApproved unchanged; :174 forwards markExecutionUnverified without ownership checks. Store worker validation applies only when both worker IDs are supplied: src/governance/approvals/approval-store.ts:957.
Reads also return mutable backing records: approval-store.ts:745 and worker-approval-port.ts:153. That undermines the cached ownership check at :168.
Minimal fix: bind identity internally, enforce it inside the locked mutation, and return detached immutable records. Add foreign-consumption, foreign-classification, and record-alias tests.

D8 — major — Invalid authority files can weaken policy or erase ownership conflicts.
src/governance/policy/policy-loader.ts:43 skips invalid rules; :54 skips unreadable/unparseable files; :59 can substitute permissive defaults. This affects the separate rule evaluator, not proof of a PolicyGate execution bypass.
src/coordination/ownership/ownership-registry.ts:97 treats every load/parse failure as an empty registry. Auto-acquisition can then grant formerly conflicting paths at :405.
Minimal fix: distinguish first-use absence from corruption/access failure; fail closed for established authority stores.

D9 — major — Validator accepts fabricated qualification and unsupported independence.
benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs:141 checks supplied runner text, :153 checks supplied counts, :206 checks nonempty negative-case text, :218 checks path existence. None establishes executed assertions or links component evidence to executed tests.
Probes accepted an imaginary runner, fabricated passing counts, package.json as every clause’s evidence, “x” negative cases, and an all-zero nonexistent commit.
Changing independence to declared-independent plus “trust me” produced QUALIFIED-INDEPENDENT through :263 and :278.
Minimal fix: require commit-bound execution receipts, assertion-specific evidence, and independently verifiable qualifier provenance. Treat semantic qualification as review work.

D10 — major — Valid HOLD can exit zero.
A partial component yields ok:true and outcome:HOLD at benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs:271. CLI exit selection at :351 returns zero for that combination.
Original author-only record genuinely exits 3; that distinction works. HOLD handling does not.
Minimal fix: return nonzero for every HOLD; test actual CLI exit codes.

D11 — major — Deny-wins claim contradicts the cited evaluator.
src/governance/policy/rule-evaluator.ts:39 returns the first matching rule. A source-loaded probe with matching allow followed by matching deny returned allow.
Separately, PolicyGate can return ask before reaching a hard capability deny: policy-gate.ts:279 and :301 precede :316.
Minimal fix: define and implement precedence consistently; test overlapping rules in both orders and hard-deny interactions with earlier ask branches.


VACUOUS ASSERTIONS

These assertions cannot establish the controls attributed to them:

- tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts:216 — “refuses to construct” constructs an ordinary port and checks method absence. No contaminated facade, no toThrow assertion. Delete the constructor guard at worker-approval-port.ts:180; this test still passes.

- tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts:160 — “does not expose another worker’s records” explicitly expects another worker’s record to be defined at :165. It proves binding lookup separation, not worker isolation.

- tests/security/pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts:79 — offenders starts empty at :84 and is never populated before assertion at :106. Walker only counts files; gate text alone is searched. An eligibility-based bypass elsewhere still passes.

- tests/security/pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts:140 — counts one spelling of a consumed assignment. A second writer using Object.assign or different syntax remains invisible. Removing locking also leaves this assertion satisfied.

- tests/security/pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts:155 — checks source regexes, not refusal behavior. Disable guards while retaining their text in comments or dead code; assertions still pass.

- tests/security/pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts:176 — approval-related test asserts only matchedRuleId at :197. It does not assert allow, deny, pause, or effects.

- tests/policy/rule-evaluator.test.ts:12 — “first matching” supplies disjoint rules, only one of which matches. First-match and last-match implementations both pass. Overlapping deny-wins is never tested.

- tests/security/approval-consumed-propagation.test.ts:122 — replay only must differ from allow. Returning ask and minting replacement approval passes. Required deny/unverified/no-reissue behavior is unasserted.

- tests/ownership/ownership-registry.test.ts:121 — persistence test reacquires the same scope for the same agent and asserts acquired:true at :137. Disable persistence entirely: fresh acquisition still succeeds. Assert retained record identity and rejection of a different agent.

- tests/ownership/ownership-gate.test.ts:143 — “continuation-resume” directly calls the helper, without a continuation or executor. Remove executor’s continuation ownership check at executor.ts:486; this test still passes.

- tests/policy/command-classifier.test.ts:20 — asserts high risk, unsafe classification, and tags. No deny decision, dispatcher, or effect exists in this test. Remove execution enforcement; it still passes.

- tests/security/policy-protected-path-parity.vitest.ts:97 and :136 — meaningful gate assertions, but no dispatch or post-denial file read. They remain green if a caller ignores the deny and writes anyway.

These are not grounds to dismiss the substantive ordinary replay assertions or the blanket-allow ownership regression test. Those would fail when their particular controls are removed.


UNDECLARED GAPS

Beyond F-02/F-03/F-04/F-07/F-11:

- Actual subprocess crash/restart, concurrent process claims, and stale-lock takeover. “Fresh process” at tests/security/legacy-approval-single-use.test.ts:139 means another instance in the same process; concurrency at :156 uses one executor.
- Safe-shell and continuation routes bypassing current policy. Continuation dispatch skips the current gate at src/capabilities/tools/executor.ts:466.
- Scoped-worker shell/dynamic-tool mutation and unrestricted approval-store reopening.
- Coordination replay classification, replacement requests, and terminal execution receipts.
- Corrupt/unreadable policy and ownership authority.
- Constructor-guard mutation coverage and worker-identity enforcement across all mutation methods.
- End-to-end denied-action effects, beyond the already declared full-TUI gap.
- Evidence provenance, semantic assertion coverage, and actual CLI HOLD behavior.

F-02’s explanation is also inaccurate: openWorkerApprovalPort accepts workspace explicitly at src/governance/approvals/worker-approval-port.ts:115. Production supplies cwd, but a wrong workspace is constructible.

Useful omitted evidence exists: tests/tools/tool-router.test.ts:779 exercises new-file ownership backstop; :807 asserts rejection and :808 checks file absence. It is neither cited nor included in the record’s runs.


CAN THESE TESTS PASS WITHOUT THE CONTROL?

- F8b — YES. Delete construction guard; facade absence tests still pass. Unrestricted store/CLI access is untested.
- Authority — YES. Safe-shell bypass already coexists with passing gate tests. Matching allow-before-deny also survives the cited evaluator tests.
- Crash/recovery — YES for cross-process recovery and coordination guarantees. Remove safe stale-lock reclamation or return ask on consumed coordination replay; cited assertions do not detect those failures. Ordinary legacy replay controls have stronger tests.
- Ownership — YES for shell confinement and router backstop. Remove file.create’s router check; cited ownership files never invoke it. Restoring the historical gate early-allow bug would fail policy-gate.test.ts:429.
- Denied-action negatives — YES. Let executor ignore a deny and invoke its router; cited classification/helper tests still pass.


VERDICT

NO. Author-side qualification is not sound enough to accept as coverage of all five stated clauses.

Most direct disqualifier: tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts:216 cannot fail when the constructor guard it claims to prove is removed. Other claimed negatives are missing, structurally vacuous, or contradicted by reachable logic.

Independent qualifier still must reproduce the runtime defects, exercise real subprocess crash/claim races, run filesystem-mutating evidence in an authorized disposable environment, mutation-test critical guards, and bind results to an exact artifact. This read-only review supplies source findings and limited execution evidence; it does not clear Gate E.
