CLAUSE VERDICTS

Review target: HEAD 19999d03cc3e5e59a7dffda2d09bffefc421c33e. Record pins ea863a25b5dd0e014d275bc1c4b979b7b7d5afee. Reviewed source/test paths showed no committed differences between those commits. GitNexus index was 41 commits stale; source supplied final evidence.

All cited test files exist. Ran 123 focused, filesystem-pure cases from existing compiled tests: command-classifier 7, rule-evaluator 13, path-scope 98, selected policy-gate 5; all passed. Did not rebuild or run fixtures that write files, execute mutation commands, or initialize durable stores. Therefore the record’s 282/282 execution claim remains independently unverified.

- F8b — PARTIAL. Facade omits decision methods; pending consumption is rejected and status remains pending: tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts:82, :90. Cross-worker outcome rejection has meaningful assertions at :195. Constructor guard exists at src/governance/approvals/worker-approval-port.ts:180, but its supposed negative test never triggers it. Neither process confinement nor exclusive access through this facade is established.

- Authority — NOT QUALIFIED. Gate-level mode tests meaningfully assert deny: tests/policy/policy-gate.test.ts:113. Runtime dispatch bypasses that gate for safe-shell commands in ask mode: src/capabilities/tools/executor.ts:513. Deny-wins for overlapping rules is also absent.

- Crash/recovery — PARTIAL. Ordinary replay tests assert denial, zero effects, and consumed/unverified state: tests/security/approval-crash-recovery.test.ts:95. However, coordination replay does not meet that contract, and stale-lock recovery admits a two-claimant interleaving.

- Ownership — NOT QUALIFIED for “every declared-scope write.” File-tool fix is real and meaningfully pinned: tests/policy/policy-gate.test.ts:429. Shell execution can bypass declared owned paths. Router backstop coverage also sits outside the cited evidence set.

- Denied-action negatives — PARTIAL. Decision assertions exist, but the four cited files do not demonstrate dispatch suppression plus absence of prohibited effects. Classification is repeatedly presented as execution evidence.


DEFECTS

D1 — major — Hard deny bypassed by safe-shell dispatch.
src/capabilities/tools/executor.ts:513 dispatches directly to downstream at :530, before authorization at :534.
Counterexample: ask mode, shell.run: deny, denyCommands: ["ls"], command "ls". A probe executing the extracted source dispatch body reached a mock router once; policy gate calls: zero.
Minimal fix: evaluate mandatory denies before bypassing approval prompts. Add executor-level denied-result and zero-router-call assertions.

D2 — major — Declared ownership does not constrain shell writes.
src/governance/policy/policy-gate.ts:94 limits owned-scope enforcement to file.create, file.delete, patch.apply. Capability allow returns at :331 for shell.run. ShellToolRouter executes arbitrary commands at src/capabilities/tools/tool-router.ts:451 without checking ownedPaths.
A gate probe with ownedPaths ["inside.txt"], shell.run allowed, and "printf x > outside.txt" returned allow. Child executor construction supplies ownedPaths but no ownership registry: src/agents/subagent-cli.ts:723.
Minimal fix: deny unscopable mutation channels for scoped workers, or enforce scope through an actual execution sandbox. Cover shell and dynamic tools explicitly.

D3 — major — Stale-lock takeover can remove a new owner’s live lock.
src/governance/approvals/approval-store-lock.ts:48 checks staleness, then unconditionally removes the directory at :49.
Two processes can observe one stale lock; the delayed contender then deletes the first contender’s replacement lock. Both can load the approved state and claim.
The ledger CAS does not necessarily prevent this: src/governance/approvals/approval-store.ts:308 obtains the latest version during commit, rather than retaining the version read with the approval.
Minimal fix: atomic conditional approved-to-consumed transition against the loaded version, plus race-safe stale-lock recovery. Add deterministic two-process contention coverage.
This is a source-derived interleaving, not an executed race reproduction.

D4 — major — Coordination replay creates replacement authority instead of denying uncertainty.
src/governance/policy/policy-gate.ts:715 recognizes only an approved exact binding. A consumed binding falls through to creation of another pending approval at :739. It is neither classified unverified nor denied.
Minimal fix: explicitly handle consumed coordination bindings before creating requests; assert deny, original unverified state, and no replacement request.

D5 — major — Preconsumed approvals lose execution receipts.
src/capabilities/tools/executor.ts:682 skips both claiming and assigning claimedApprovalId when approvalConsumed is true. Outcome recording at :875 requires that variable.
Consequently, gate-consumed coordination approvals can remain pending after successful execution.
Minimal fix: retain the approval ID for both consumption routes; test through executor, effect, terminal receipt, and replay.

D6 — major — Worker facade is not an exclusive approval-authority boundary.
Normal wiring uses openWorkerApprovalPort: src/agents/subagent-cli.ts:708. But that function itself imports unrestricted ApprovalStore at src/governance/approvals/worker-approval-port.ts:119.
Child spawning provides ordinary process execution under the same user, without an OS authority boundary: src/agents/subagent-manager.ts:364. Approval CLI directly calls store.resolve without worker-origin checks: src/interfaces/cli/commands/approval.ts:106.
A child capable of executing JavaScript or invoking that CLI can reopen the store. The facade narrows an object; it does not confine a compromised process.
Minimal fix: explicitly limit the claim’s threat model, or move decision authority behind parent-owned IPC/storage permissions inaccessible to children.

D7 — major — Worker identity is not enforced across the facade’s mutation surface.
src/governance/approvals/worker-approval-port.ts:162 forwards consumeApproved unchanged; :174 forwards markExecutionUnverified without ownership checks. Store worker validation applies only when both worker IDs are supplied: src/governance/approvals/approval-store.ts:957.
Reads also return mutable backing records: approval-store.ts:745 and worker-approval-port.ts:153. That undermines the cached ownership check at :168.
Minimal fix: bind identity internally, enforce it inside the locked mutation, and return detached immutable records. Add foreign-consumption, foreign-classification, and record-alias tests.

D8 — major — Invalid authority files can weaken policy or erase ownership conflicts.
src/governance/policy/policy-loader.ts:43 skips invalid rules; :54 skips unreadable/unparseable files; :59 can substitute permissive defaults. This affects the separate rule evaluator, not proof of a PolicyGate execution bypass.
src/coordination/ownership/ownership-registry.ts:97 treats every load/parse failure as an empty registry. Auto-acquisition can then grant formerly conflicting paths at :405.
Minimal fix: distinguish first-use absence from corruption/access failure; fail closed for established authority stores.

D9 — major — Validator accepts fabricated qualification and unsupported independence.
benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs:141 checks supplied runner text, :153 checks supplied counts, :206 checks nonempty negative-case text, :218 checks path existence. None establishes executed assertions or links component evidence to executed tests.
Probes accepted an imaginary runner, fabricated passing counts, package.json as every clause’s evidence, “x” negative cases, and an all-zero nonexistent commit.
Changing independence to declared-independent plus “trust me” produced QUALIFIED-INDEPENDENT through :263 and :278.
Minimal fix: require commit-bound execution receipts, assertion-specific evidence, and independently verifiable qualifier provenance. Treat semantic qualification as review work.

D10 — major — Valid HOLD can exit zero.
A partial component yields ok:true and outcome:HOLD at benchmark/evidence/gate-e-qualification-record.mjs:271. CLI exit selection at :351 returns zero for that combination.
Original author-only record genuinely exits 3; that distinction works. HOLD handling does not.
Minimal fix: return nonzero for every HOLD; test actual CLI exit codes.

D11 — major — Deny-wins claim contradicts the cited evaluator.
src/governance/policy/rule-evaluator.ts:39 returns the first matching rule. A source-loaded probe with matching allow followed by matching deny returned allow.
Separately, PolicyGate can return ask before reaching a hard capability deny: policy-gate.ts:279 and :301 precede :316.
Minimal fix: define and implement precedence consistently; test overlapping rules in both orders and hard-deny interactions with earlier ask branches.


VACUOUS ASSERTIONS

These assertions cannot establish the controls attributed to them:

- tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts:216 — “refuses to construct” constructs an ordinary port and checks method absence. No contaminated facade, no toThrow assertion. Delete the constructor guard at worker-approval-port.ts:180; this test still passes.

- tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts:160 — “does not expose another worker’s records” explicitly expects another worker’s record to be defined at :165. It proves binding lookup separation, not worker isolation.

- tests/security/pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts:79 — offenders starts empty at :84 and is never populated before assertion at :106. Walker only counts files; gate text alone is searched. An eligibility-based bypass elsewhere still passes.

- tests/security/pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts:140 — counts one spelling of a consumed assignment. A second writer using Object.assign or different syntax remains invisible. Removing locking also leaves this assertion satisfied.

- tests/security/pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts:155 — checks source regexes, not refusal behavior. Disable guards while retaining their text in comments or dead code; assertions still pass.

- tests/security/pre-r032-non-bypass.vitest.ts:176 — approval-related test asserts only matchedRuleId at :197. It does not assert allow, deny, pause, or effects.

- tests/policy/rule-evaluator.test.ts:12 — “first matching” supplies disjoint rules, only one of which matches. First-match and last-match implementations both pass. Overlapping deny-wins is never tested.

- tests/security/approval-consumed-propagation.test.ts:122 — replay only must differ from allow. Returning ask and minting replacement approval passes. Required deny/unverified/no-reissue behavior is unasserted.

- tests/ownership/ownership-registry.test.ts:121 — persistence test reacquires the same scope for the same agent and asserts acquired:true at :137. Disable persistence entirely: fresh acquisition still succeeds. Assert retained record identity and rejection of a different agent.

- tests/ownership/ownership-gate.test.ts:143 — “continuation-resume” directly calls the helper, without a continuation or executor. Remove executor’s continuation ownership check at executor.ts:486; this test still passes.

- tests/policy/command-classifier.test.ts:20 — asserts high risk, unsafe classification, and tags. No deny decision, dispatcher, or effect exists in this test. Remove execution enforcement; it still passes.

- tests/security/policy-protected-path-parity.vitest.ts:97 and :136 — meaningful gate assertions, but no dispatch or post-denial file read. They remain green if a caller ignores the deny and writes anyway.

These are not grounds to dismiss the substantive ordinary replay assertions or the blanket-allow ownership regression test. Those would fail when their particular controls are removed.


UNDECLARED GAPS

Beyond F-02/F-03/F-04/F-07/F-11:

- Actual subprocess crash/restart, concurrent process claims, and stale-lock takeover. “Fresh process” at tests/security/legacy-approval-single-use.test.ts:139 means another instance in the same process; concurrency at :156 uses one executor.
- Safe-shell and continuation routes bypassing current policy. Continuation dispatch skips the current gate at src/capabilities/tools/executor.ts:466.
- Scoped-worker shell/dynamic-tool mutation and unrestricted approval-store reopening.
- Coordination replay classification, replacement requests, and terminal execution receipts.
- Corrupt/unreadable policy and ownership authority.
- Constructor-guard mutation coverage and worker-identity enforcement across all mutation methods.
- End-to-end denied-action effects, beyond the already declared full-TUI gap.
- Evidence provenance, semantic assertion coverage, and actual CLI HOLD behavior.

F-02’s explanation is also inaccurate: openWorkerApprovalPort accepts workspace explicitly at src/governance/approvals/worker-approval-port.ts:115. Production supplies cwd, but a wrong workspace is constructible.

Useful omitted evidence exists: tests/tools/tool-router.test.ts:779 exercises new-file ownership backstop; :807 asserts rejection and :808 checks file absence. It is neither cited nor included in the record’s runs.


CAN THESE TESTS PASS WITHOUT THE CONTROL?

- F8b — YES. Delete construction guard; facade absence tests still pass. Unrestricted store/CLI access is untested.
- Authority — YES. Safe-shell bypass already coexists with passing gate tests. Matching allow-before-deny also survives the cited evaluator tests.
- Crash/recovery — YES for cross-process recovery and coordination guarantees. Remove safe stale-lock reclamation or return ask on consumed coordination replay; cited assertions do not detect those failures. Ordinary legacy replay controls have stronger tests.
- Ownership — YES for shell confinement and router backstop. Remove file.create’s router check; cited ownership files never invoke it. Restoring the historical gate early-allow bug would fail policy-gate.test.ts:429.
- Denied-action negatives — YES. Let executor ignore a deny and invoke its router; cited classification/helper tests still pass.


VERDICT

NO. Author-side qualification is not sound enough to accept as coverage of all five stated clauses.

Most direct disqualifier: tests/security/worker-approval-port.vitest.ts:216 cannot fail when the constructor guard it claims to prove is removed. Other claimed negatives are missing, structurally vacuous, or contradicted by reachable logic.

Independent qualifier still must reproduce the runtime defects, exercise real subprocess crash/claim races, run filesystem-mutating evidence in an authorized disposable environment, mutation-test critical guards, and bind results to an exact artifact. This read-only review supplies source findings and limited execution evidence; it does not clear Gate E.
